Chapter 1

Introduction

Welcome to BERNINA artista, the leading embroidery design
application for home users. BERNINA artista software is a
Windows-based product incorporating many of the conventions
with which most PC users are already familiar.

First-time users

Upgrade users

If you are new to BERNINA artista, you’ll be
pleased with its flexible digitizing capabilities,
the ease with which you can create and edit
designs, and how simple it is to add or create
your own lettering. However, before you begin
working with the product, read the Digitizing
Embroidery Designs. This chapter presents an
overview of the basic concepts of digitizing with
BERNINA artista and provides you with
important guidelines on how to use this
software to achieve the best results.

BERNINA artista is a greatly-enhanced version
of our earlier products. It has many new and
expanded features which make digitizing easier
and more efficient. This manual contains
revised step-by-step instructions that show you
how these features are incorporated. As many
of the digitizing methods are more advanced,
read the chapter Digitizing Embroidery Designs
to familiarize yourself quickly with the scope of
these improvements.

You should also familiarize yourself with Basic
Procedures. Here you will learn how to start the
application, create or open a design, print
designs, and how to use some of the essential
tools and features that BERNINA artista
provides. The chapter Viewing Designs also
provides an overview of the design viewing
modes in BERNINA artista.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Access to the BERNINA artista software is
controlled by a security device connected to the
computer. Upgrades can be made easily by
entering new access codes or replacing the
external security device (‘dongle’). See Entering
security codes for details. The software now
provides improved security protection with new
dongles.

BERNINA artista product levels
The BERNINA artista product levels are
described below.

Editor
With BERNINA artista Editor you can edit your
existing designs and add lettering. With this
system you can:

 Modify designs or combine different designs.
 Scale and reshape your designs.
 Add lettering to any design. There is a large

range of embroidery alphabets to choose
from.
 Even edit individual stitches to fine-tune your
designs to a perfect finish.
 Change the color sequence for better
stitch-outs.

 Use scanned or imported artwork to digitize
designs on-screen.
 In addition to the automatic digitizing tools,
use specialized digitizing tools, plus a wide
range of artistic, decorative fill patterns.
 Create appliqué with Auto Appliqué.

 Convert any Windows TrueType font to an
embroidery alphabet.

 Save all your favorite settings to templates.
Caution The product levels and optional extra
capabilities are controlled by the security
device. This device is the most important and
valuable part of your system and should be
treated with care.

BERNINA artista documentation

Auto Designer

BERNINA artista documentation includes:

With BERNINA artista Auto Designer you can
create attractive designs with a minimum of
fuss from scanned artwork. It has all the editing
and lettering features of BERNINA artista Editor,
plus powerful automatic digitizing features such
as Auto Digitizer, Magic Wand and PhotoSnap.
With this system you can:

 User Manual
 Online Help
 Quick Reference Guide
 Support Notes
 Installation Notes

 Use scanned or imported artwork to digitize

User Manual

designs on-screen.
 Use Auto Digitizer to create attractive
embroidery designs automatically from
scanned artwork.
 If you want greater control, use Magic Wand
to interactively convert pictures to stitches.
 Use PhotoSnap to turn your photographs into
line stitching.

automatic and manual digitizing tools and

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

The User Manual is produced in both printed and
online form. The online version is accessed from
the Help menu. The printed manual is complete
with hundreds of step-by-step instructions as
well as illustrations and screens to guide you
through the digitizing process.
Because you can pick from a range of BERNINA
artista product levels, as well as add options and
upgrades as your interest grows, everything you
need to know about your system is contained in
a single User Manual.
The first four chapters in this manual (including
this one) contain general information that
should be of interest to all readers.

Conventions used in the manual
The following conventions are adopted and used
throughout the manual.

Shortcut

Description

C+S

While holding down the Control key
(Ctrl), press the lowercase letter S
key.

C+V+H

While holding down the Control key
(Ctrl), press Shift and the H key.

For a complete list of keyboard shortcuts, refer to the
Quick Reference Guide.

Commands
In this manual, commands on a meu or
submenu are referred to by both the menu and
command names. For example the ‘Show All’
command in the ‘View’ menu is referred to as
View > Show All.

Dialog boxes
Dialog boxes are referred to as ‘dialogs’ and are
shown in the manual only if they provide
important information on using BERNINA
artista. The screen images provided are
intended to be representations, not exact
duplicates of the layouts generated by the
software.

Chapter 2

Digitizing Embroidery Designs

BERNINA artista provides embroiderers with
a fast, flexible way of creating and editing
embroidery designs. This section provides an
overview of the embroidery digitizing process
as it applies to BERNINA artista.
The BERNINA artista embroidery digitizing
process will consist of some or all of the
following stages:

 planning designs
 digitizing designs
 modifying designs
 improving design quality
 adding lettering
 stitching out designs

Planning designs

Good embroidery quality

To create good quality embroidery, you need to
choose suitable artwork for use as a digitizing
backdrop. Unless you are an experienced
digitizer, do not use artwork which is
complicated. Artwork that is not in digital format
needs to be scanned. Possible sources for good
embroidery design artwork include:

Good embroidery quality starts with the design.
You then need a good quality machine to stitch
it out. But even that is not enough if you do not
use the correct fabric, threads, backings,
tension, and so on. Keep the following points in
mind when planning your design:

 books
 internet or CD clipart
 original artwork

 design looks good — shapes, colors, balance
 stitches are neat, smooth and even
 shapes are filled with correct fill and outline
stitches

 stitches are angled to match shapes

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

 shapes are stitched correctly — no unwanted  details are clearly defined
gaps
 lettering is clear and easy to read

The stitched-out design should also have the
following characteristics:

 The design stitches out efficiently on the
machine.

 The fabric has not puckered around the
stitched areas.

 The design is free of loose ends.

Design sequence
The design sequence is the order in which
objects are created and therefore the order in
which they are stitched out. You can change the
sequence of objects in the design to improve
sewing — for example, to minimize color
changes. Details should be stitched last. See
Resequencing embroidery objects for details.

Preparing to digitize

2

Before you start digitizing, you need to analyze
and plan design shapes and stitching sequence
carefully.
Design shapes need to be clearly defined to
make them easy to embroider. The easiest
shapes to embroider have a relatively constant
width, with smooth edges, no sharp turns and
no small, protruding details.
Simplify edges in artwork
before or during digitizing

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

1

details
last

Stitch types
Decide which stitch types you will use for each
design shape. While you normally set stitch type
before digitizing each shape, BERNINA artista
lets you change them at any stage. See Outline
and Fill Stitches for details.

8

Chapter 2: Digitizing Embroidery Designs
Use grid lines and hoop to help accurately align
or size embroidery objects. You can show or
hide the grid and hoop. There are many hoop
sizes available to suit the size of your design.
See Displaying the grid and Displaying the hoop.
Fill stitches

When working with embroidery designs, you
need to understand the stitching sequence. You
can check a design’s stitching sequence by
‘traveling’ through it by stitches, colors or
objects. You can also check the sequence by
slowly redrawing your design on screen. See
Viewing the stitching sequence for details.

Outline stitches

Digitizing designs
Digitizing allows you to build designs from basic
shapes. Different tools are suited to creating
different shapes. Digitize shapes manually by
marking reference points along a shape’s
outline or use the automatic digitizing tools
which create shapes automatically.
Designs created in BERNINA artista are
composed of embroidery ‘objects’ which have
their own unique sets of ‘properties’. The most
important property of an embroidery object is
its stitch type.
While you are creating your design, you can
view it in different modes:

 Design mode: open, digitize and edit all your
designs.

 Artistic View: see what your design looks like
after it has been stitched.

Design mode

Depending on the shape and stitching you
require, you use different tools to enter
‘reference points’ with Open Curve, Closed
Curve, Block Digitizing, Circle/Oval or Rectangle
tools.

Open curve
Use the Open Curve tool to
digitize lines that do not form
an enclosed shape. You can
only use Outline stitch with
this tool. See Digitizing open
shapes for details.

Closed curve

See BERNINA artista ‘views’ for details.

Picture mode

Digitizing tools

Use the Closed Curve tool to
digitize shapes that form an
enclosed shape. You can use
Outline or Fill stitch with this
tool. See Digitizing closed
shapes for details.

Artistic View

Zoom in on an area to see individual stitches or
more detail, view the design at actual size or
zoom out to see the whole design. See Zooming
in and out for details.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

9

Chapter 2: Digitizing Embroidery Designs

Block Digitizing

Single and triple outline

Use the Block Digitizing tool to
digitize columns of varying
width and stitch angle.
Digitized pairs of reference
points define the outline,
while lines connecting the
pairs define the stitch angles.
See also Digitizing columns of
varying width.

Single outline stitch is used
for thin lines and outlines of a
shape. Triple outline stitch is
used when you want the
outlines of a shape to be
thicker. See Creating outlines
with Single and Triple Outline
stitch for details.

Circles
Use the Circle/Oval tool to
digitize circles. You can use
Outline or Fill stitch with this
tool. See Digitizing circles and
ovals for details.

Rectangles
Use the Rectangle tool to
digitize squares and
rectangles. You can use
Outline or Fill stitch with this
tool. See Digitizing squares
and rectangles for details.

Outline and fill stitches
There are two stitch types available in BERNINA
artista: Outline and Fill. The type of Outline or
Fill stitch you select will depend on:

 the shape — is it simple or complex?
 the size — how big or small is it?
 the appearance — what effect are you trying
to create?

An Outline Stitch is used for single lines, for
outlines of shapes and for borders. A Fill Stitch
is used to fill the shapes in a design. See Outline
and Fill Stitches for details.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Satin outline
Satin stitch is used for thick
borders or outlines. See
Creating outlines with Satin
stitch for details.

Blanket outline
Blanket stitch is widely used
for tacking appliques as a
column around the border.
The stitches form a comb
pattern. See Creating outlines
with Blanket stitch for details.

Pattern outline
Pattern stitch is used to create
decorative borders or
outlines. See Selecting and
inserting pattern runs for
details.

Blackwork Border
Blackwork embroidery was
traditionally hand stitched
using black silk thread on
white linen fabric. It can be
used to decorate articles of
dress or furnishings such as
hankies, table napkins,
tablecloths, and doilies. See
Adjusting Blackwork Border outlines for details.

10

Chapter 2: Digitizing Embroidery Designs

Backstitch

Step fill

Backstitch is an old,
adaptable stitch which can be
used as a delicate outline.
This stitch follows intricate
curves well if the stitches are
small and even, so that they follow the flow of
the curve. Backstitch can be used for redwork
designs. See Adjusting Backstitch outlines for
details.

Step Fill is suitable for filling
large, irregular shapes to give
the appearance of a solid field
of stitching. BERNINA artista
offers a variety of step
patterns which will bring
texture and interest to your
embroidery designs. See
Creating fills with Step fill for
details.

Stemstitch
Stemstitch is a detail stitch
that can be used to outline
items. It is used for stems
and vines with other
decorative stitches, or as an
outline for stitches such as
satin fill or motifs. See Adjusting Stemstitch
outlines for details.

Candlewicking Border
Candlewicking Border is a
detail stitch that can be used
to outline areas in a design.
See Adjusting Candlewicking
Border outlines for details.

Satin fill
Satin stitch is well-suited to
stitching narrow shapes. Satin
stitch creates a glossy,
high-quality effect. Special
Satin is used mainly for
shapes that are too wide to be
stitched with Satin. See
Creating fills with Satin fill for details.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Fancy fill
Fancy Fill is used like Step Fill.
BERNINA artista provides a
variety of exciting, creative
fills. See Creating fills with
Fancy fill for details.

Blackwork Fill
Blackwork gets its name from
the black silk thread
traditionally used in this form
of embroidery. The most
common blackwork fills
employ simple stitches to
create complex scrolling or geometric patterns.
By using different blackwork fill patterns, you
can create light and dark tones in a design.
Blackwork Fill can only be applied to closed
curve or rectangular filled objects. See
Adjusting Blackwork Fills for details.

Candlewicking Fill
Candlewicking is a traditional
white-on-white embroidery
technique, usually done on
white linen or cotton fabric
with heavy cotton threads.
More use of color is becoming
popular today. Stitches used are mostly knots,
both Colonial and French, as well as stem stitch.
Candlewicking can be used to embroider white
bedspreads, pillows and clothing. Candlewicking

11

Chapter 2: Digitizing Embroidery Designs
Fill can only be applied to closed curve or
rectangular filled objects. See Adjusting
Candlewicking Fills for details.

Lacework
Lacework stitch consists of an
open square trellis-like
pattern which gives objects a
lacey look. Lacework can only
be applied to closed curve or
rectangular filled objects. With Lacework fills
you can adjust the layout of the preset patterns.
Use the same method as for pattern fills. See
Adjusting Lacework fills for details.

In BERNINA artista, you can change the
background color of the design window to match
the color of your fabric. Or you can select a
background fabric for more realistic previews
and presentations. See Changing background
colors or fabrics for details.

yellow background

denim background

Stippling Fill
Stippling is a method of
creating a fill made of run
stitches which meander
around within a border. It can
be applied to closed curve or
rectangular filled objects. You
can control stitch density in
Stippling objects by adjusting the stitch length
and the loop spacing. See Adjusting Stippling
fills for details.

Digitizing with artwork
To create good quality embroidery, you need to
choose suitable artwork for use as a digitizing
backdrop. You can display a backdrop while you
digitize, or hide it temporarily. See Digitizing
with backdrops for details.

Object properties and templates
When you start a new design, BERNINA artista
uses the default settings or ‘values’ stored in the
template. Most designs use the ‘Normal’
template, but you can create and use your own.
See Creating design templates for details.

Thread colors and thread charts
You need to decide how many different thread
colors to use and the sequence in which they will
be stitched. When you digitize, you select
thread colors from the color palette. Wherever
possible, simplify the design to reduce the
number of color changes. See Thread Colors
and Thread Charts for details.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

original artwork

required parts traced

Artwork that is not in digital format needs to be
scanned correctly to produce good quality
images. See Scanning artwork into BERNINA
artista for details.
You may also need to clean up complex images
in a graphics program before inserting them into
BERNINA artista. See Editing images in a
graphics package for details.

Modifying designs
When you have digitized your design you can
modify it as a whole, edit individual objects and
even individual stitches.
You can combine designs by
inserting one file into
another. See Combining
objects for details.
Connectors link objects in a
design. See Connecting
embroidery objects for
details.
Stitching sequence usually occurs in the order in
which the design was digitized. However, you
can change this by a variety of methods. See
Resequencing embroidery objects for details.

Digitize complete images automatically with
Auto Digitizer. See Digitizing with Auto Digitizer
for details.

Arranging and transforming objects

Editing stitches

You can change the
position, size and
orientation of objects in a
design by moving, scaling
and transforming them.
Group objects together to
apply a change to them all
at once. You can also
create multiple copies in a wreath shape using
Mirror Merge. See Arranging and Transforming
Objects for details.

You can use Stitch Edit to
select stitches. You can move
individual and multiple
stitches. You can add stitches
to fill gaps and delete stitches.
See Editing Stitches for
details.

scaled down
20%

original object
scaled up 20%

Reshaping objects
BERNINA artista lets you reshape objects with
control points. See Reshaping objects for
details.
With fill objects you can set a stitch angle for the
entire object or you can add multiple stitch
angles with the Stitch Angles tool. See Adjusting
stitch angles for details.

object with 90° stitch angle

multiple stitch angles

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Improving design quality
Embroidery stitches pull the
fabric inwards where the
needle penetrates. This can
cause the fabric to pucker,
and gaps to appear in the
embroidery. Use underlay and
pull compensation to achieve
smooth, even placement of
stitches, and eliminate gaps in
your design. See Improving Stitch Quality for
details.
Larger areas and stretchy fabrics such as knits
and pique need more underlay than smaller
areas and firm fabrics such as drill or leather.
See Adjusting fabric settings for details.

Adding appliqué
You can automatically create
all the stitching you need for
appliqué using Auto Appliqué.
See Creating appliqué objects
using Auto Appliqué for
details.
You can create appliqué objects with partial
cover stitching to create an overlapping effect
without doubling-up borders. It is important to
plan your design carefully beforehand so that
you know where the appliqué cover stitch needs
to be and where it is not needed. See Creating
partial cover appliqué objects for details.

14

Chapter 2: Digitizing Embroidery Designs

Artistic stitch effects

Travel on Edges

You can create a variety of artistic stitch effects
in BERNINA artista.

The Travel on Edges effect
creates very open fill
stitching, often used as a
background or for filling large
shapes. Use Travel on Edges
with the Gradient Fill effect to
eliminate travel stitches inside
an object. This creates a
smoother stitch fill with sparse stitching. Use
Travel on Edges with the Color Blending feature
to eliminate travel stitches inside a blended
embroidery object. See Creating open fills for
details.

Textured Edge
Use Textured Edge to create
rough edges on objects, to
create shading effects, or to
imitate fur or other fluffy
textures in your design. See
Creating textured edges for
details.

Wave Effect
The Wave Effect is a feature
that allows you to curve fill
stitch along a line to create a
flowing stitch effect. The
stitches follow the digitized
line but maintain uniform
density and needle
penetration patterns.
Wave Effect can be applied to Closed Curve
objects using Step or Fancy fill stitch types. To
apply the effect to new Closed Curve objects,
select Wave Effect before digitizing. See
Creating wave effects for details.

Gradient Fill
Gradient Fill varies the stitch
spacing between dense and
open fill, producing shading
and color effects which are
difficult to achieve manually.
See Creating gradient fills for
details.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Color Blending
The Color Blending effect
automatically creates
different shading,
perspective,
three-dimensional and color
effects by blending two
colored layers in an
embroidery object. See
Creating color blending effects for details.

Buttonholes
BERNINA artista allows you to
insert preset buttonholes, to
specify the size of buttonholes
and merge the buttonhole
into a design.
Buttonholes are usually
formed with satin column
stitches bordering a slit that is approximately
the length of the button's diameter. The satin
stitch border protects the slit from fraying. The
buttonhole consists of a bead of satin stitches
with a bar tack. See Creating buttonholes for
details.

15

Chapter 2: Digitizing Embroidery Designs

Adding lettering

Monograms

You can add lettering to
designs quickly and easily
then edit directly on-screen.
You can apply formatting to
lettering objects in the same
way as a word processor,
including italics, bolding, and
right/left justification. See Adding lettering to
embroidery designs and Formatting lettering for
details.

You can create a design made
up of initial letters, known as
a monogram, quickly and
easily with BERNINA artista.
Diamond2 and Diamond3 are
special monogram alphabets.

Special characters and symbols
You can add special
characters and symbols
directly through the Select
Symbols dialog or by means
of the Windows Character
Map.

Baselines
You can apply horizontal, vertical, and curved
baselines to your lettering objects. See Applying
lettering baselines for details.

Elastic lettering
Using ‘elastic lettering’ you
can also distort lettering
shapes. See Creating special
effects with Elastic Lettering
for details.

TrueType fonts
You can create new alphabets by converting
TrueType fonts. See Converting TrueType fonts
to embroidery fonts for details.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Cutting holes with lettering
You can cut holes in selected Closed Curve or
Rectangle objects with a single stitch angle
using the Lettering Remove Overlay tool. See
also Cutting holes with lettering.

Adding cross stitch
Using BERNINA artista Cross
Stitch, you can add cross
stitches either to an entire
design or selected areas.
Cross stitch is sometimes
combined with appliqué. See
Cross Stitch Essentials for
details.
Use the full range of cross stitch types to create
outlines and fills of any color. You can also
control the orientation of fractional cross
stitches to create different patterning effects.
See Selecting cross stitch types for details.
Cross stitching is a popular technique for filling
large areas with low stitch counts. It can also be
used for outlines and borders. You can adjust
the density of any cross stitch by stitching it
once, twice or three times. See Setting the
stitch density for details.

16

Chapter 2: Digitizing Embroidery Designs

Magic Box

thread count 2

thread count 6

You can move, rotate or mirror blocks of cross
stitches as well as lock cross stitches and/or
outlines so that they are not moved by accident.
See Cross Stitch Editing for details.
Pictures can be inserted or scanned into
BERNINA artista Cross Stitch for use as
digitizing backdrops. You can digitize backdrop
images automatically using Auto Stitch. You can
also select blocks of color in your picture to
stitch using Magic Wand. See Digitizing filled
shapes with Magic Wand for details.
BERNINA artista Cross Stitch lets you add
lettering to a design by typing it directly in the
design window. See Digitizing cross stitch
letters for details.

Design processing
By default BERNINA artista saves designs to its
native file format, ART. This format contains all
information necessary both for stitching a
design and for later modification. See
Processing Files of Different Formats for details.

The Magic Box reads and
writes designs saved in other
formats. You can load a
design from a design card
into BERNINA artista, edit it
then write it to a Magic Card.
The Magic Box easily converts designs to and
from other formats. You can also use Magic Box
with BERNINA artista to edit your design before
writing it onto a card. See Magic Box for details.

Design Management
BERNINA artista Portfolio provides an efficient
way for managing embroidery designs. This
design management tool can access design files
stored on your computer hard disk, CD-ROM, or
floppy disk. Portfolio even lets you archive and
view design files in folders which have been
archived by means of the popular WinZip utility.
It recognizes all design file formats used by
BERNINA artista.
Catalogs of designs in a folder contain
thumbnails and/or summary information. They
can be sent to a printer, published on your
website or intranet, and exported in formats
suitable for spreadsheet and database
programs. See Design Management for details.

Stitching out and storing designs
You can output embroidery designs in a variety
of ways — saving to a design card, floppy or
hard disk, or sending directly to machine for
stitching. Designs stored on a designs card or
your computer can also be retrieved and
customized in BERNINA artista. You can also
print a hard copy of designs. See Outputting and
Storing Designs for details.

Chapter 3

Basic Procedures

To start using BERNINA artista, you
need to know the basic procedures,
such as starting up, opening and
creating designs, and saving. Other
basic procedures include showing and
hiding the grid, displaying and using
toolbars and switching to Artistic View.
Security access codes control your
access to special options in the
software. You need to be able to
identify your system’s identity codes,
and enter new access codes for
upgrades.
This section describes how to start
BERNINA artista, how to open designs, start new ones and use the basic commands. It
also explains how to turn on and off the grid and measure distances on-screen. The
section also explains the procedure for entering security codes.

Starting BERNINA artista
Double-click to start BERNINA artista.

Open BERNINA artista using the desktop icon or
the Windows Start menu.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

To start BERNINA artista

 Double-click the BERNINA artista shortcut
icon on the Windows desktop.

Alternatively, select Programs > BERNINA
artista > BERNINA artista from the Start
menu.

Using commands
Once you start BERNINA artista, you use
commands or icons, and dialogs to complete
your tasks. You select commands in BERNINA
artista in the same way as other Windows
applications — from menus or toolbars.
Keyboard shortcuts are also available for the
most frequently used commands. See BERNINA
artista Shortcut Keys for details.

Showing or hiding toolbars
Toolbars provide quick and easy access to
BERNINA artista commands. BERNINA artista
toolbars are dockable. This means you can
move them around the design window, or hide
them as convenient.
Tip To increase your working area, hide
unwanted toolbars and use the menu and
keyboard commands instead.

To show or hide a toolbar

 To move a toolbar to a more convenient
location, click and drag it.

 To dock it in its normal position, double-click
the toolbar title.

 Click the X in the top right corner of the
repositioned toolbar to hide a toolbar.

 To show closed toolbars, press Shift + P.

formats. You can also save designs to floppy

Saving designs

Saving a design records its file name, location
and format, and updates it with any changes
you make. When you save an existing design
under a new name, to a different location or

Magic Box for details.
Files saved in ART format are automatically
compressed when saved and decompressed
when re-opened. This reduces the storage
space required, and makes it possible to save
large files to floppy disk, or send them as email
attachments.

Measuring distances on-screen
Use Measure (View menu) to measure distances
on-screen.
Measure the distance between two points and
the angle of the line between them using the
Measure command. Measurements are shown in
millimeters or inches, depending on the
measurement system selected in the Windows
Control Panel. See your Windows
documentation for more information.
Tip For more accurate results, zoom in before
you measure. The measurement is always the
actual size, and is not affected by the zoom
factor.

 Click the Show Grid icon again to turn off the
grid display.

Displaying the hoop
Click Show Hoop (General toolbar) to view
the hoop. Right-click to change the settings.
The large hoop is displayed when you open
BERNINA artista. The hoop represents the
embroidery hoop that you connect to your
BERNINA home embroidery machine. See also
Working with Hoops.

Y=).

 Length of the measured line (L=).
 Angle of the line relative to the horizontal
(A=).

Entering security codes
Use Security (Settings menu) to enter security
codes.
BERNINA artista is controlled by a security
device or ‘dongle’ attached to the computer, in
conjunction with security access codes entered
into the software. The dongle number is the
most important number. See the Installation
Notes for details.
Each dongle has a unique number on the tag
which is attached to your dongle. When
referring to your system in correspondence or
by telephone, you need to quote the dongle
number.

Chapter 4

Viewing Designs

BERNINA artista provides many viewing features to make it easier to work with your
design. Zoom in on an area to see more detail or view the design at actual size. Preview
an existing design in different colors on different fabrics.

BERNINA artista also provides information about designs in a variety of ways and
formats. Before even opening BERNINA artista or your design, you can check some
design information for ART files directly from Windows Explorer. You can view stitching
details about a design in the Design Properties dialog. Also the Print Preview provides
essential design information, including a design preview, the size of the design, color
sequence and any special instructions.
Show or hide various design elements with the available display settings. You can show
or hide needle penetration points and the stitches themselves.
This section explains the design viewing modes available in BERNINA artista as well as
the various design viewing settings. It also explains how to obtain and customize
information about your designs.

Design viewing modes
BERNINA artista provides a number of distinct
viewing modes. You can also zoom in on an area

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

to see more detail, view the design at actual
size, as well as zoom out to see the whole
design.

1 In Design Mode, select Edit > Stitch Edit or

Viewing the stitching sequence
When working with embroidery designs, you
need to understand the stitching sequence. You
can check a design’s stitching sequence by
‘traveling’ through it by stitches, colors or
objects. You can also check the sequence by
slowly redrawing your design on screen.
BERNINA artista simulates stitching out by
changing stitches from black to their allocated
thread colors as they are ‘stitched’.

Traveling by stitches
Use the stitch travel tools or shortcut keys to
travel through the design one or more stitches
at a time. See Summary of travel functions for
details.
The current needle position is indicated by a
large white cross or ‘needle position marker’.
Initially, this is located at the end of the design.
When you travel through stitches, the needle
position marker moves accordingly. The current
stitch number appears in the Status Line.

Chapter 5

Selecting Objects

When working with embroidery designs, you need to
understand the stitching sequence. You can check a design’s
stitching sequence in BERNINA artista by ‘traveling’ through
it stitch-by-stitch. You can also check the sequence by slowly
redrawing your design on screen.
BERNINA artista provides various ways to select the objects
that comprise an embroidery design. You can select all
objects to modify the design as a whole, or individual objects
for more precise modification.
This section describes how to check designs by traveling
through them. It describes how to select objects using the
selection tools and keyboard. It also shows how to select while traveling through the
design, or by using the Color Film. You also see how to select objects of a specific color.

Selecting all objects in a design
Select all objects to apply changes to a whole
design. See also BERNINA artista Shortcut Keys.

To select all objects in a design

 Select Edit > Select All or press Ctrl + A.
Sizing handles appear around the entire
design.

all objects selected

Chapter 6

Working with Hoops

Fabrics should be hooped before stitching out on the
machine. If the design is complex or will not fit within one
of the included hoops, you may need to create your own
custom hoop. See Creating custom hoops for details.
This section describes how to set the hoop center, change
the hoop size, rotate hoops, create and edit custom hoops.
See also Displaying the hoop.

Setting the hoop center
Click Show Hoop (General toolbar) to turn
hoop display on or off. Right-click to change
hoop settings.
BERNINA artista makes sure that your design is
always centered in the hoop, but you can use
the manual option if you need to digitize in
another part of the hoop. You can also change
the center position of the hoop.

Chapter 7

Digitizing Methods

In BERNINA artista, you build designs from basic shapes or
‘embroidery objects’. Like drawing objects, embroidery
objects have certain defining characteristics or ‘properties’
such as color, size, position, and so on. They also have
properties unique to embroidery such as stitch type and
density.
The most important property for an embroidery object is its
stitch type. Different stitch types are suited to different
shapes. See Outline and Fill Stitches for details.
The process of creating embroidery objects on-screen is
called ‘digitizing’. Like creating designs in graphics
applications, this involves the use of different digitizing
tools. Digitizing tools in BERNINA artista are similar to
drawing tools except that the end result is an embroidery
object rather than a drawing object.
Object properties are defined as you digitize, but they can
be changed at any stage. When you create an embroidery object, you can accept the
default settings for the particular digitizing method, or apply new ones. Default settings
are stored in the design template. You can also define ‘current’ properties to influence
all the objects you create in the current design. See Object Properties and Templates for
details.
This section describes how to digitize shapes with the main digitizing methods. It also
explains how to adjust digitizing settings to obtain the best results.

Digitizing tools
Different digitizing methods or ‘tools’ are suited
to creating different shapes or design elements.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Digitizing methods divide broadly into two
categories — outline and fill.

57

Chapter 7: Digitizing Methods

Tip Drawing objects themselves can be
converted to embroidery objects using
BERNINA artista Magic Wand. See Digitizing
with Magic Wand for details.

Selecting digitizing tools
Use Open Curve (Design toolbar) to digitize
a line.
Use Closed Curve (Design toolbar) to digitize
an irregular shape.
Use Circle/Oval (Design toolbar) to digitize a
circle or oval.
Use Rectangle (Design toolbar) to digitize a
square or rectangle.
Use Block Digitizing (Design toolbar) to
digitize columns.
Digitizing methods divide broadly into two
categories — outline and fill. BERNINA artista
provides various tools with which to digitize
shapes — the Open Curve, Closed Curve, Block
Digitizing, Circle/Oval, and Rectangle. The Open
Curve tool is used to create outline shapes. The
Block Digitizing tool is used to create columns of
varying width and stitch angle. The Closed
Curve, Circle/Oval, and Rectangle tools are used
to create filled or outlined shapes.

Reference points and control points
Once you have selected a digitizing tool, you
digitize shapes in the same way by marking
‘reference points’ along an outline. In general,
you mark a reference point where:

 a curved outline changes curvature
 the outline has corners
 the outline changes from a straight line to

You always use the left mouse button to mark a
corner point, and the right mouse button to
mark a curve point. Two points marked with the
left mouse button are always connected with a
straight line. Two points marked with the right
mouse button are always connected with a
curved line.
The reference points you mark when digitizing
the shape become the ‘control points’ of the
selected object. Control points appear on object
outlines and are used to edit or ‘transform’
objects. Such actions may include reshaping,
scaling (resizing), letter spacing, changing entry
and exit points.
Key to control points
= entry point
= exit point
= corner point
= curve point
= stitch angle line

Most control points can be added, deleted,
moved or changed to either corner or curve
points. Some control points have a specific
function and cannot be deleted — for example,
the entry point marker.

Switching between fill and outline
digitizing methods
Use Outline (Stitch and Color toolbar) to
digitize a line.
Use Fill (Stitch and Color toolbar) to digitize a
shape.
You can quickly switch from fill stitch to outline
stitch using the Fill and Outline tools. See also
Outline and Fill Stitches.

curve.

Digitizing open shapes
Use the Open Curve (Design toolbar) to
digitize open shapes.
Use the Open Curve tool to digitize open shapes
of single/triple run, satin, blanket, patterned or
craft stitching. Open Curve places a row of
stitches along a digitized line. You can only use
an outline stitch with this tool. This tool is
typically used to add borders to designs.
Create objects using left and right mouse clicks
to mark reference points to form an outline. Use
left-clicks to enter corner points and right-clicks
to enter curve points.

Tip Follow the prompts in the status bar to
help you digitize. If you make a mistake,
press Backspace to delete the last reference
point, then continue digitizing.

Digitizing closed shapes
Use Closed Curve (Design toolbar) to digitize
irregular closed shapes.
Digitize complex shapes with the Closed Curve
tool. Create objects using left and right clicks to
mark reference points to form the boundary
outlines.

Chapter 8

Outline and Fill Stitches

All embroidery objects in BERNINA artista have defining settings
or ‘properties’. Certain properties, such as size and position, are
common to all. Others are specific to the object type. The most
important property is the stitch type. Whenever you create or
modify an object, stitches are generated according to the
associated stitch type. Settings such as spacing and length can
also be adjusted to suit.
BERNINA artista provides a variety of digitizing methods or ‘tools’
for creating embroidery objects. The tools divide broadly into two
types — ‘outline’ and ‘fill’. The same applies to stitch types.
Outline and fill stitch types are defined whenever you create
embroidery shapes, but they can be changed at any stage.
This section explains how to select an basic outline and fill stitch
types, as well as change stitch settings to obtain the best results.

Selecting or changing stitches
BERNINA artista provides a number of basic
outline and fill stitch types. It also provides
artistic and craft stitches for special uses. See
also Artistic Stitch Effects, Pattern Runs and
Fills, and Craft Stitch Borders and Fills.

Tip Preset stitch values by changing the current
properties in the Object Properties dialog before
you digitize. See Object Properties and
Templates for details.

Different stitch types suit different shapes.
Whenever you create an object, it always takes
the currently selected stitch type. You can
change this at any stage.

Adjusting Satin outline stitch spacing
and width
Click Object Properties (General toolbar) to
adjust stitch length and spacing.
Stitch spacing is the distance in millimeters
between two needle penetrations on the same
side of a column. Where a column is narrow,
stitches are tight, thus requiring fewer stitches
to cover the fabric. Where a column is very
narrow, stitches need to be less dense because
too many needle penetrations can damage the
fabric. See also Adjusting Satin outline stitch
spacing and width.

Satin spacing

Change the stitch density in Satin fills by
adjusting the stitch spacing setting in the Object
Properties dialog. The larger the spacing
between stitches, the lower the density. The
smaller the spacing, the higher the density.

stitch

The Object Properties > Outline Stitch tab

Blanket stitch is used to create long, narrow
columns with a ‘comb’ effect. It is often used to
stitch borders around appliqués. The needle
penetrates both sides of the column, and then
penetrates the second side again, laying the
thread along the side. Blanket stitches are
usually more open than Satin stitches.

Satin fill is well-suited to stitching narrow
columns and shapes, where the length of each

stitches are almost parallel, with every second
stitch slightly slanted. Because there are
generally no needle penetrations breaking up

effect.

Chapter 9

Object Properties and Templates

The properties of embroidery objects define general characteristics
such as size and position, as well as embroidery-specific
characteristics such as stitch type and density. The particular stitch
settings determine how stitches will be regenerated when you
reshape, transform or scale an object.
When you create an embroidery object, you can accept default
settings for the particular digitizing method, or apply new ones.
Default settings are stored in the design template. You can also
define ‘current’ properties to influence all the objects you create in
the current design.
This section explains how to change the object properties in your
design, as well as how to apply, create and maintain styles and
templates in BERNINA artista.

Applying and managing object
properties
BERNINA artista stores three sets of object
properties: default, current and existing.
Note Some object properties can be modified
on-screen; for example, you can change the
size properties by scaling the object with the
selection handles. Other properties, such as
stitch spacing or length, are modified in the
Object Properties dialog.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Default object properties
Default object properties are the properties
stored in a design template and become the
starting settings used when creating a new
design based on that template.

Current object properties
Current object properties are the settings used
to create new objects. Unless you change these
settings, they remain the same as the template
defaults. You generally change them to save
time when digitizing. For example, you may
preset the Step stitch spacing setting to use a
specific density for all new Step objects you
create.

75

Chapter 9: Object Properties and Templates

Note The change affects new objects in the
design, not the template itself. To apply changes
to all new designs based on the current
template, you need to change the default — not
the current — properties.

Tabs display at the top of the Object
Properties dialog. These provide access to all
the possible object properties settings.

2 Click a tab to view the current settings and
change as required.

3 Click OK.

Properties of existing objects
Properties of existing objects are the settings
stored with each object in the design. You can
make the properties of a selected object into
new current properties or new defaults. You can
also apply current properties to existing objects.

Setting current object properties
Use Object Properties (Settings menu) to set current
properties.

Changing properties of selected objects
You can change the properties of a selected
object or objects. For example, if you make a
design bigger, you may want to stitch some
objects with Step Fill instead of Satin Fill for
better coverage. If you select more than one
object, the Object Properties dialog will only
display tabs that include relevant settings for all
selected objects.

When you change current object properties, the
new settings automatically apply to any new
objects created in the current design. You can
also apply current properties to existing objects.
Before starting, you can adjust the current
object properties to new settings.

To set current object properties

Note You can also create a new template

Managing design templates
Templates are special files used to store styles
and default settings. Use templates when
digitizing frequently-used design types so that
you do not have to set the current properties
every time. For example, a template may
include standard objects and sample lettering.
It may simply have preferred stitch settings,
lettering font and size, and colors set as current
properties. Or it may have special density, pull
compensation or underlay settings set up to suit
different fabrics.

Chapter 10

Thread Colors and Thread Charts

When you digitize, you select thread colors for each object
you create from the color palette in the design window. The
color palette contains a selection of thread colors tailored for
each design. This color scheme represents the actual thread
colors in which a design will be stitched.
You can preview a design in different colors and on different
fabrics. In effect, you can preview multiple color schemes.
You can also print multiple color schemes, design
backgrounds, and icons of color blocks with the print preview.
For each color scheme, you can select colors from commercial
thread charts containing a range of colors from different
thread manufacturers, or define your own. To save time when setting up new color
schemes, you can create your own thread charts, using the most frequently used colors.
You can add, edit and delete threads from a thread chart, or rename or delete the chart
itself. In BERNINA artista you can search for particular threads by various criteria.
This section describes how to select colors from the color palette. There is also an
explanation of how to define your own thread colors and charts.

Changing thread colors
New objects are digitized using the selected
color in the color palette. You can change the
color before or after digitizing. If the color you
want is not available in the color palette, you
may need to add it. See Adding your own colors
to thread charts for details.

Note Due to the differences in computer
monitors and computer video cards, the
approximate colors displayed should be used
only as guides. Threads should always be
matched to the appropriate physical color card.

current color

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

81

Chapter 10: Thread Colors and Thread Charts

Selecting a new current color
When you digitize a new object, it automatically
takes the color selected in the color palette. This
is the ‘current’ color.

To select a new current color

from the color
palette

Matching thread colors
In BERNINA artista you can search for particular
threads manually by various criteria. Thread
color matching lets you to find and change a
thread color based on closest match in one or
several thread charts.

a thread.

Digitizing with Artwork

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Chapter 11

Digitizing with Backdrops

Artwork in both bitmap and vector format can be loaded,
pasted or scanned into BERNINA artista for use as
digitizing templates or ‘backdrops’. Textured backgrounds
can also be imported to show what a design will look like
on real fabric. Almost any artwork can be used —
photographs, magazine pictures, clipart drawings, and
even fabric samples.
From within BERNINA artista you can open images in MS
Paint, Corel PHOTO-PAINT®, or Paint Shop Pro™. Images
updated in this way are automatically re-imported into
BERNINA artista.
This section describes how to choose suitable artwork for
digitizing purposes as well as how to scan it into BERNINA artista. It also describes how
to digitize with images as backdrops, as well as how to show and hide them as you
digitize.

Using backdrops
Backdrops can be used as a guide to manual
digitizing. Alternatively, BERNINA artista
semi-automatic and automatic digitizing can
convert bitmaps to embroidery designs. In
summary, backdrops can help you to:

 Digitize objects manually.
You trace shapes and lines over the artwork
using the appropriate digitizing tools. See
Digitizing Methods for details.
 Digitize shapes automatically with Magic
Wand.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

You select a shape and Magic Wand
automatically determines the required
stitches.
 Digitize complete images automatically with
Auto Digitizer.
You select the image and Auto Digitizer
automatically determines the shapes and
stitches needed to digitize the design. See
Digitizing with Auto Digitizer for details.
 Digitize photographs with PhotoSnap.

93

Chapter 11: Digitizing with Backdrops
Again you select the image and PhotoSnap
automatically determines the shapes and
stitches needed to digitize the design. See
Creating embroidery designs from
photographs for details.

Choosing artwork for digitizing
There are two broad categories of artwork file:

drawing

 Vector drawings: These consist of outlines

which may be colored and may have colored
fills. Vector outlines remain thin and clear
even at large zoom factors, so you can
digitize them accurately.
 Bitmap images: These consist of colored dots
or pixels. When you zoom in on a small area,
the outlines become jagged and are
displayed as a series of colored squares or
pixels.
To create good quality embroidery, you need to
choose suitable artwork. For both manual and
automatic digitizing purposes, ‘clean’ artwork
with a limited number of solid colors and
well-defined outlines work best. Once in
BERNINA artista, you can set general properties
such as size and position. You can also arrange
and transform artwork in the same way as
embroidery designs. See also Supported
drawing and image formats.

digitized with Magic
Wand tool

Bitmap images
Bitmap images can be loaded or scanned into
BERNINA artista for use as digitizing backdrops.
For both manual and automatic digitizing
purposes, ‘clean’ images, sometimes referred to
as ‘cartoons’, work best. Such images have a
limited number of solid colors which in turn have
well-defined outlines. Ideally, they are:

 well defined, where each shape is made up of
pixels of the same color

 clearly ‘blocked’, where each shape is a
stitchable size, at least 1 sq mm

 saved at a color depth of at least 256 colors

(8 bit), or preferably millions of colors (16
bit). (Images are automatically reduced to
256 colors or less when loaded into BERNINA
artista.)

Vector drawings
Vector drawings can be loaded into BERNINA
artista for use as digitizing backdrops. They are
automatically converted to bitmap images.

Tip You can scale and transform bitmap images
once they are loaded into BERNINA artista.
However, if an image needs to be resized or
rotated, it is best to do so during scanning.
Scaling afterwards may distort the image.

Automatic digitizing
BERNINA artista automatic digitizing techniques
— Auto Digitizer and Magic Wand — produce
best results with images of the type found in

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

94

Chapter 11: Digitizing with Backdrops
clipart libraries or created from scratch in a
graphics package. See Digitizing shapes
automatically with Magic Wand and Digitizing
with Auto Digitizer for details.

simulate colors that are missing from an image
palette.

Automatic digitizing can work with images from
other sources but they require greater or lesser
amounts of preparation. This is because most
commonly available images are not made up of
solid colors. Scanners introduce noise, while
graphics packages perform ‘dithering’ and
‘anti-aliasing’ to improve image print quality.

dithered color
blocks

Automatic digitizing works least effectively with
photographic images which may contain many
dithered colors and complex forms. With
photographs, however, you can pick out shapes
that you want to embroider, leaving out
unnecessary detail. Alternatively, PhotoSnap is
available to create embroidery designs from
scanned photographs.

Like noisy images, dithered images need to be
color-reduced before use. Be aware, however,
that while the software is excellent at
processing dithered colors within a defined
outline, it does not work so well with
non-outlined images. See Preparing images for
automatic digitizing for details.

Scanned images

Anti-aliased images

Images scanned from
hardcopy drawings or
existing embroidery
typically contain a lot of
introduced ‘noise’.
While they can be used
as input to automatic
image containing a lot of
digitizing, once again,
scanner ‘noise’
best results are
achieved with relatively
clean images consisting of solid color blocks.
Typically, logos and simple drawings scanned
from business cards, letterheads, books,
magazines, cards fall into this category.

Anti-aliasing is a
software technique
similar to dithering
which is used to soften
hard outlines where
color blocks intersect.
It produces smoother
outlines by ‘blurring’
the pixels where colors
join.

Noisy images typically need to be prepared by
reducing the color count and sharpening the
outlines. See Preparing images for automatic
digitizing for details.

Dithered images
Dithering is a software technique which
combines existing colors in a checkerboard
arrangement of pixels. It is typically used to

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

anti-aliased outlines

Where anti-aliasing is
deliberately used to blur outlines, these need to
be ‘sharpened’ before use with automatic
digitizing. See Preparing images for automatic
digitizing for details.

Scanning artwork into BERNINA
artista
If you have existing artwork, you can scan it
with a TWAIN-compatible scanner using the
BERNINA artista scanning function. It is

95

Chapter 11: Digitizing with Backdrops
important to scan your artwork properly if you
intend to use one of the automatic digitizing
techniques; the scanned image quality will
affect the quality of the final embroidered
design.

good image: good stitch
quality

poor image: poor stitch
quality

Preparing artwork for scanning
With embroidery design, less is more. You do
not need every detail in an image to create a
design. You use the ‘structure’ of the image
rather than the fine details of texture and color.
To simplify artwork, you can cover it with tracing
paper and draw only the essential shapes and
lines which will be filled with stitches. When
scanning, take away the original artwork and
put white paper behind the tracing paper.

original artwork

required parts traced

Shiny surfaces, such as glossy photographs,
may not scan well. Cover them with tracing
paper. If the artwork has very light colors,
highlight outlines with a fine black felt-tip pen.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Scanning resolution
Most scanners require you to enter scanning
resolution information. Resolution determines
the number of dots per inch (dpi) used to create
a drawing. The higher the value, the clearer the
image but larger the file. For digitizing
purposes, use a maximum resolution of 300 dpi
(dots per inch). A resolution of 72dpi (screen
resolution) will usually be sufficient. Generally
speaking, the smaller the source image and/or
more detail it contains, the higher the resolution
needs to be. Use the following table as a guide.
Type of artwork

Scanning
resolution

Business cards, letter heads

150 - 300 dpi

Hand sketches

150 - 300 dpi

Photos and images

150 - 300 dpi

Commercial art, line drawing

72 - 150 dpi

Color mode
Most scanners also require you to enter color
mode information. First decide whether your
image is line art (black and white drawing),
sketch, color picture, or black and white or color
photograph, then choose an appropriate mode.
Black and white mode produces the smallest
files. Color photograph and grayscale modes
generate 256 color images and produce similar
sized files. ‘RGB’, ‘True Color’ or ‘millions of
colors’ modes generate 16.7 million colors and
produce the biggest files. Use the table below to
decide which mode is suitable for use with your
image.
Note Note that scanners may use different
terms for the same mode, some of these terms
are included.

Sharpening

See Setting up scanners for details.

Some scanning software lets you apply what is
called ‘sharpening’ as you scan. Sharpening
compensates for the slight blurring in a scanned
image by looking for any differences between
colors in the image. Sharpening accentuates
these differences which makes the image edges
more defined. It does not increase the image
details; it just makes them more obvious. In
general, use sharpening with images that have
well-defined outlines. Do not use it with
non-outlined images.

Chapter 12

Processing Images for Automatic
Digitizing

BERNINA artista supports the automatic and semi-automatic
digitizing of bitmap images. The quality of the resulting designs
greatly depends on the type and quality of the original artwork. In
order to make bitmap images more suitable for automatic digitizing,
BERNINA artista also provides image processing capabilities and
links to graphics packages.
This section describes how to prepare images for automatic
digitizing. It explains how to prepare both outlined and non-outlined
images.

Preparing images for automatic
digitizing
Before applying automatic digitizing, you
frequently need to improve or ‘clean up’
artwork. To work effectively, both Auto Digitizer
and Magic Wand require solid color images as
input. You can improve artwork both with
bitmap editing tools in graphics packages
and/or the image processing tools provided in
BERNINA artista. In fact, the software will not
let you apply Auto Digitizer until the image has
been suitably processed.

Outlined vs non-outlined images

outlined and non-outlined. Outlined images
ideally have a solid black outline around each
colored area. Non-outlined images ideally
consist of solid areas of color. Outlined and
non-outlined images require different methods
of preparation.

image with outlines

image without outlines

Before preparing your image you need to know
what type you are using. For the purposes of
automatic digitizing, there are two categories —

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

103

Chapter 12: Processing Images for Automatic Digitizing

Image clean up
In practice, cleaning up scanned images may
involve any one or a combination of the
following techniques:

 reducing the number of colors
 adding or emphasizing outlines
 removing noise, dithering or anti-aliasing
 eliminating unnecessary detail
 cropping sections
 eliminating backgrounds.
Color reduction
Sometimes an image looks clean but extra
colors have been introduced during scanning or
in a graphics package. Color reduction means
reducing the actual number of image colors in
order to eliminate unnecessary detail and
reduce each block to a single color. Color
reduction also cleans the image, removing noise
and anti-aliasing if present. This in turn helps
minimize the number of trims and color changes
required in the resulting embroidery design.
Reduce colors in a non-outlined image using the
Artwork Preparation tool and in an outlined
image using the Outlined Artwork Preparation
tool.

If you are scanning images, make sure you scan
them correctly for best results. See Scanning
artwork into BERNINA artista for details.

image scanned in RGB
color mode

image scanned in 256
color mode

Be aware that the Artwork Preparation tool is
good at removing noise and anti-aliasing but not
so good at processing dithering in non-outlined
images. By contrast, the Outlined Artwork
Preparation tool is excellent at processing
dithered colors as it averages all pixels within a
defined outline.

Artwork Preparation
tool used with outlined
image — dithered color
blocks not completely
cleaned, edges fuzzy

Color reduction should only be applied if the loss
of detail does not affect the image shapes.

Noise filtering
Noise filtering means restoring the solid color
blocks of the original artwork in scanned
images. This is achieved by merging different
shades into one solid color. Noise filtering is
important for automatic digitizing because it
makes it easier for the software to identify solid
color blocks which become embroidery objects
in the resulting design. It also cleans up blurred
or mottled areas of color.

Chapter 13

Automatic Digitizing

The Magic Wand tool is useful for quickly creating embroidery
objects from scanned images that do not require particular
artistic effects or embroidery-specific knowledge. This in turn
frees you to spend more time on the artistic or inherently
complicated areas of your designs.
BERNINA artista Auto Digitizer automatically converts artwork
to fully digitized embroidery with little or no intervention.
Various forms of artwork can be used— both bitmap and
vector — and various levels of user ‘assists’ applied to the
process.
With Auto Digitizer, novice embroiderers can create simple
designs quickly and easily. Similarly, experienced
embroiderers can improve productivity when working with simple designs.
Use PhotoSnap to create embroidery from photographs and other images.
This section describes how to automatically convert bitmap images to embroidery
designs, and create embroidery from photographs. It also describes how to digitize
shapes automatically with the Magic Wand and Auto Digitizer.

Digitizing with Magic Wand
The Magic Wand tool provides everything
necessary to digitize shapes in bitmap images
automatically.

when inserted into the software, it will need to

software will not let you apply automatic

Creating embroidery designs with Auto

Digitizing with Auto Digitizer
An extension of Magic Wand technology, Auto
Digitizer recognizes shapes in artwork and
makes decisions about the most suitable stitch
types to use. It also determines the stitching
sequence based on closest join. Artwork is
effectively ‘batch processed’ to create the many
embroidery objects that make up a design.
Auto Digitizer allows you some control over how
an image is interpreted during conversion. You
can choose to omit selected colors and you can
specify stitch types. Once created, you can
always edit the final result using the available
digitizing techniques. See also Digitizing
Methods. Always remember to apply underlays
and you may need to reshape some objects. See
Selecting underlays for details.
Note Even if your artwork looks ready to stitch
when inserted into the software, it will need to
be image-processed before conversion. The
software will not let you apply automatic
digitizing techniques without preliminary
image-processing. See Processing Images for
Automatic Digitizing for details.

In essence, creating an embroidery design with
Auto Digitizer is simply a matter of selecting the
image you want to convert, and clicking the
Auto Digitizer tool. There are, however, some
settings you can adjust to optimize the
conversion process for a particular image.
Only one image may be selected at a time. The
command is disabled if the selection contains
anything other than an image.
Note Change the pull compensation to 0.4mm
when using Auto Digitizer.

To create embroidery designs with Auto
Digitizer

1 In Picture mode, scan or load an image.
See Scanning artwork into BERNINA artista
for details.

2 Select the image and process it.
See Processing Images for Automatic
Digitizing for details.

3 Click the Auto Digitizer icon.
The Auto Digitizer dialog opens.

Chapter 14

Combining and Resequencing Objects

BERNINA artista lets you add to designs quickly by duplicating
and copying existing objects. It also lets you combine designs by
inserting the contents of one file into another.
When working with embroidery designs, you need to understand
the stitching sequence. You can check a design’s stitching
sequence in BERNINA artista by ‘traveling’ through it
stitch-by-stitch. You can also check the sequence by slowly
redrawing your design on screen. Stitching sequence usually
occurs in the order in which the design was digitized. However,
you can change this by a variety of methods.
This section describes how to combine objects and designs by copying and pasting,
duplicating, and inserting techniques. It also describes how to travel through a design
as well as resequence objects by cut and paste, by color and with the Color Film.

Combining objects
A design or design objects can be copied or cut
and placed on the Windows clipboard for
temporary storage. It can then be pasted any
number of times, either within the same or
another design, until replaced on the clipboard.
You can also cut, copy and paste lettering
objects within and between designs.

Copying and pasting objects
Click Copy (General toolbar) to copy selected
objects to the clipboard.
Click Paste (General toolbar) to paste copied
objects in the design.
You can copy objects to create multiple,
identical objects, or to insert objects from other
designs.
Note You can also remove objects from a
design using the Cut command and paste them
back in again. Cut and Paste changes the

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

123

Chapter 14: Combining and Resequencing Objects
stitching sequence in the design. See
Resequencing objects with cut and paste for
details.

To copy and paste objects

2 Click the Copy icon.

objects.
Objects can be duplicated rather than copied.
When an object is duplicated, it is not copied to
the clipboard. This leaves the clipboard free for
you to cut or copy other objects.

clipboard.

Chapter 14: Combining and Resequencing Objects

Connecting embroidery objects
Connectors link objects in a
design. They can be jumps or
run stitches. Settings are
applied automatically to
generate connectors and
tie-offs.
The default connectors in
BERNINA artista are jumps.
Ideally, you should plan your
embroidery so that connecting
stitches will be covered later by
other elements of the design. If
a connecting stitch is not
covered, you need to trim it after
stitching the design. Connector
stitches are generated
automatically but you can
resequence objects or change
the start and end points to
minimize the number of trims.
Tie-ins and tie-offs are extra
stitches that are added before
and after long jumps and color
changes to stop the stitches
unravelling.

short jump
connector (solid
line)
long jump connector
(dotted line)

connector showing
color change

travel run

Jumps
Jumps link objects in a design and are displayed
on screen as dashed lines. Jumps move the
frame from one part of the design to another
without needle penetrations. You generally need
to trim the connecting thread.
Jump connectors are displayed in Design mode
either as a dotted or solid line. Solid lines are
used for short connectors.

Travel runs are run stitches that connect parts
within an object. They are covered by the top
stitches.

Types of connectors
Connectors can be jumps or run stitches.
Connectors are displayed only in Design View,
they are hidden in Artistic View. Short jumps are
shown as a solid line, long jumps display as
dotted lines. Color changes are shown as
dash-dotted lines. Connectors are inserted
automatically in your design.

short connector: solid line

Dotted lines are used when there is a color
change and when the connector is long (more
than 12.7mm).

Tip Press Shift + C to show or hide connectors in
Design View.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

126

Chapter 14: Combining and Resequencing Objects

long connector: dotted line
connectors display in Design View

a color change: dash-dot line

Long jump connectors have needle jumps of
7mm along their length. This will cause the
machine to stop a few times while moving the
hoop slowly to the next needle penetration
point. Jump connectors are not displayed in
Artistic View.

start and end points adjusted: fewer connectors

Note The default connector jump length is the
same as that set for Auto Jump.

connectors NOT displayed in Artistic View

Tie-ins
Tie-in stitches are automatically inserted at the
start of objects to prevent stitches from
unraveling. They are inserted inside the shape
on the second stitch.

Travel runs
Travel runs are added when the needle has to
travel between points within an object, a line of
run stitch is used under the main stitches. They
are actually stitched so you need to make sure
they are covered by top stitches.
tie-in at the start of the object

Tie-ins are automatically added when the
previous jump connector is longer than 3mm
(2mm for lettering) and after a color change.
Tip Travel through the design by 1 stitch to
check for tie-ins.

Tie-offs
With jumps, the stitch needs to be tied off so the
thread can be trimmed without the stitches
unravelling. Tie-offs are automatically added
when the next jump connector is longer than
3mm (2mm for lettering) and before a color
change.

Resequencing embroidery objects
The embroidery objects in a design form a
stitching sequence. Initially, objects are stitched
in the order in which they were created. You can
change the position of a selected object by
cutting it, then pasting it somewhere else in the
sequence, or by using the Resequence
command. You can also resequence objects by
color or using the Color Film.

check for tie-offs.

Automatic sequencing
Use Blackwork Run (Craft Stitch toolbar) to
automatically sequence selected embroidery
objects.
The Blackwork Run feature lets you digitize like
objects — e.g. the fingers of a hand, sections of
a leaf — without having to think about the most
efficient stitching sequence and joins.
Blackwork Run is designed for use with outlines
made up of objects that overlap. Apply
Blackwork Run to join selected outlines to form
a single ‘automatically sequenced outline’. All
component outlines are grouped and selectable
as one.

Editing automatically sequenced objects
Automatically sequenced objects remain
editable. They can be reshaped. There is only
one entry and one exit point, but all component
objects have individual reshape points. The
object properties of a automatically sequenced
object can be accessed via the Object Properties
dialog. Stitch types can also be re-assigned via
the toolbar.

Applying automatic sequencing
Use Blackwork Run (Craft Stitch toolbar) to
automatically sequence selected embroidery
objects.
Apply Blackwork Run to selected objects. These
become a single automatically sequenced
object.

Properties of automatically sequenced
objects
Blackwork Run works with embroidery outlines
consisting of one or a combination of the
following — Open Curve, Closed Curve,
Circle/Oval, or Rectangle tools which use single
or triple outline, stem stitch or backstitch.
Outlines with Pattern Run stitch types cannot be
used. Only one each of the selected objects’
outline stitch types will be applied to the
automatically sequenced object. The stitch type
becomes that of the first object selected. The
stitch effects are also those of the first selected
object.
Note When dissimilar objects are selected and
automatically sequenced, any which cannot be
automatically sequenced are omitted.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

To apply automatic sequencing

1 Select the objects.
Note The function is only available if more
than one object of any suitable type is
selected.

Chapter 15

Arranging and Transforming Objects

BERNINA artista allows you to change the position, size and
orientation of objects in a design by moving, scaling and
transforming them. Group objects together to apply a change
to them all at once. You can modify objects directly on-screen,
or in the Object Properties dialog.
This section describes how to position objects, how to group
and align objects, as well as how to scale, rotate, skew, and
mirror objects. It also shows you how to create multiple copies
in a wreath shape using Mirror-Merge.
The scalability and stitching quality of a design ultimately depends on its original source
– ART Grade A, ART Grade B, ART Grade C, or ART Grade D. Only native ART Grade A
designs contain the complete set of design information required for 100% perfect scaling
and transformation. See Embroidery design formats for details.

Positioning objects
Position objects in your design using the mouse
to drag them to a new position, nudging them
with the arrow keys or by specifying the X:Y
coordinates in the Object Properties dialog.

To position objects using click and drag

1 Select the object/s to move.
2 Click and drag the object to a new position.

Positioning objects using click and drag
The simplest way to move an object in your
design is to click and drag it to a new position.
Use the arrow keys to ‘nudge’ objects into
position.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

cross-hair cursor
centers objects

133

Chapter 15: Arranging and Transforming Objects

3 For more accurate positioning, press the
arrow keys to ‘nudge’ the object into the
required position.
Tip Zoom in to make it easier to make small
adjustments. The distance the object moves
depends on the current zoom factor. The
greater the zoom factor, the smaller the
distance moved.

Grouping objects
Click Group (Arrange toolbar) to group
selected objects.
You can group selected objects or the whole
design to keep them together for moving,
scaling and transforming actions.

ensuring that the change is consistent across

Chapter 16

Reshaping and Editing Objects

BERNINA artista lets you modify object shapes, stitch
angles and entry and exit points by means of control
points. Stitch angle lines and entry and exit markers all
appear around selected objects. Control points vary
slightly with the object type.
Before modifying any design, a good practice is to save
a copy under a new name and keep the original in case
you want to discard your changes and start again.
This section describes how to reshape objects with
control points, reshape circles, adjust stitch angles—including multiple stitch
angles—and change entry and exit points.

Reshaping objects
Use Reshape Object (Design toolbar) to
adjust the control points of selected objects.
You can change the shape of an object by
selecting it with the Reshape Object icon and
moving, adding or deleting control points on the
outline. For some objects, you can also change
control points from corner points to curves.

Reshaping objects using control points
Click Reshape Object (Design toolbar) to
display the control points of selected objects.
Change object shapes by selecting them with
the Reshape Object icon and modifying the
available control points.

To reshape objects using control points
Note The Reshape icon lets you modify shapes
without affecting the stitch angles.

1 Select an object.
2 Click the Reshape Object icon.
Control points appear around the object.

Chapter 17

Editing Stitches

With BERNINA artista designs, stitches are
automatically generated from design outlines
and properties. This means you can scale,
transform and reshape BERNINA artista
designs without affecting stitch density or
quality.
However, BERNINA artista also lets you edit
individual stitches. You simply select them like
any other object and move the needlepoint
position as required. For example, you can
insert stitches in an object to fill gaps. You can
move or delete individual or clusters of
selected stitches.
You may need to do this particularly when working with ‘stitch’ files which do not contain
design outline data. Where possible, however, edit the object properties rather than
individual stitches. See also Embroidery design formats.
This section deals with selecting and editing stitches.

Selecting stitches

Selecting stitches by needle point

The Stitch Edit feature lets you select single
stitches, several stitches, or a range of stitches
by selecting their needle points, or dragging a
bounding box around them. Selected stitches
are highlighted in a different color.

Use Stitch Edit (Edit menu) to select individual
stitches for editing.
You can select individual stitches in Stitch Edit
mode by selecting their needle points.
Tip Zoom in and display the needle points for
easier selection.

Chapter 18

Lettering Essentials

Add lettering to designs quickly and easily, either
on-screen using the current settings, or via the Object
Properties - Lettering dialog. Lettering can be edited
directly on-screen or with the dialog. You can apply
formatting to lettering objects in the same way as a word
processor, including italics, bolding, and right/left
justification. Both interactive and numeric techniques are
available for scaling and spacing lettering. See Alphabet
Samples for details.
This section describes how to add and edit lettering,
change formatting settings, and adjust lettering size and spacing.

Adding lettering to embroidery
designs
You can add lettering to a design by typing it
directly in the design window, or entering it in
the Object Properties - Lettering dialog.

Use Lettering (Design toolbar) to add lettering
directly on-screen.
If it is not essential to fit letters precisely to a
certain area, you can type them directly
on-screen. You can also modify lettering objects
directly on-screen to achieve various artistic
effects.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Adjusting Lettering Layout for details.

Setting letter justification

When you first create lettering, it may be too big
or too small. Size can be adjusted in three ways:

 by scaling on-screen with the Select Object
or Reshape Object tools

 by adjusting width and height settings in the
Lettering tab
 by adjusting width and height settings in the
General tab.

Justification is the way that lettering aligns itself
on a baseline. You can justify lettering left or
right, center it, or fully justify it. Full justification
spreads the letters out to fill the length of the
baseline.

BERNINA artista gives you both interactive and precise
numeric control over many attributes affecting lettering
objects. You can adjust lettering objects as a group as
well as the individual letters comprising a lettering
object.
You can apply horizontal, vertical, and curved baselines
to your lettering objects. You can modify baseline type,
length, radius and angle, as well as baseline position.
You can numerically define the rotation angle of letters
relative to the baseline or the design.
This section describes how to adjust lettering objects as
well as individual letters. It also describes how to apply
and adjust lettering baselines.
