Selecting baselines
Right-click Lettering (Design toolbar)
baselines and adjust baseline settings.

Applying lettering baselines
Baselines determine the shape of lettering
objects in a design. You can place lettering on a
straight horizontal or vertical line, curve
lettering around a circle or arc, or digitize your
own baseline. Different reference points are
needed depending on the baseline you use. You
can digitize baselines on-screen.

You can select different baselines through both
the Object Properties – Lettering and Layout
dialogs. You can also adjust baseline settings.
You can apply baselines to new or selected
objects.
Tip Create identical baselines by duplicating or
copying them in your design.

To select baselines

1 Double-click a selected lettering object or
right-click Lettering.

The Object Properties - Lettering dialog
opens.
select a
baseline

2 Select a baseline.

Baselines use default settings to determine their
size, spacing and angles. BERNINA artista gives
you both interactive and precise numerical
control over many baseline settings. Techniques
are available to modify baseline type, length,
radius and angle, as well as baseline position.
Note BERNINA artista will remember which
baseline you used last and use this one if you
type letters directly on-screen.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

The baseline you choose depends on the
effect you want to achieve. You need to
digitize different reference points depending
on the type of baseline you select. Options
include:

 Straight Horizontal. See Creating
horizontal baselines for details.

 Circle CW. See Creating circle baselines

for details.
 Circle CCW. See Creating circle baselines
for details.
 Any Shape. See Creating custom
baselines for details.
 Vertical. See Creating vertical baselines
for details.

173

Chapter 19: Adjusting Lettering Layout

 Multiple. See Creating custom baselines
for details.

3 Click OK.
Creating horizontal baselines
Straight Horizontal baselines do not have a fixed
or pre-determined length. They extend as long
as you keep adding letters.

Tip Vertical lettering best suited to uppercase
for Western languages because descenders in
lowercase letters are not accommodated in the
letter spacing.

To create vertical baselines

1 Right-click the Lettering icon to open the
Object Properties - Lettering dialog.

2
3
4
5

letters.

1 Right-click the Lettering icon to open the
Object Properties - Lettering dialog.

2
3
4
5

Enter your text in the text entry box.
Select the Straight Horizontal baseline icon.
Click OK.
Click on-screen where you want to place the
letters.

Creating circle baselines
Use Circle CW and Circle CCW baselines to place
letters around a full circle. You can place the
lettering clockwise (CW) or counter-clockwise
(CCW) along the circle. By default, letters are
positioned above the Circle CW and below Circle
CCW baseline. They are also centered by
default.

Vertical baselines are straight, vertical
baselines. Vertical baselines do not
have a fixed length, and extend to fit
the letters you enter.

For a circle you need to mark two reference
points, while for an oval you need to mark three.
The position of the second reference point of the
circle or oval determines the justification point
of the text. The text is centered around this
point.

Line spacing is calculated horizontally
while letter spacing is calculated
vertically. Letters, by default, are
centered along vertical lines. New lines
are placed by default from right to left
to suit Asian languages.

Make a circle large enough for the amount and
size of lettering you have set. If the circle is too
small, or the lettering is too big, the lettering
will be crowded. Follow the directions in the
status bar. See Reshaping baselines on-screen
for details.

Creating vertical baselines

Vertical baselines are effective for
embroidering on sleeves, as a decorative effect,
and for Asian text.

Adjusting baselines
Baselines can be modified after placement
either directly on-screen or via the Object
Properties - Lettering dialog. Baselines allow
you to set the final length of the finished
lettering. Once you have placed a baseline,
BERNINA artista attempts to fit all the letters
without altering the baseline length. As the
baseline is filled with letters, the letter spacing
decreases. Eventually, letters may overlap. If
so, you can adjust the baseline so that it will

176

Chapter 19: Adjusting Lettering Layout
accommodate them, or change the width of the
letters themselves.

baseline length. Reshape Any Shape baselines

Chapter 20

Adjusting Lettering Stitch Settings

Like all embroidery objects, each lettering object
has its own properties. Before you create a
lettering object, you may want to adjust the values
for the stitch type you are going to use.
Alternatively you may want to change the stitch
values and effects of existing lettering objects.
BERNINA artista also gives you precise control
over the stitch angles of individual letters.
This section describes changing lettering stitch
types in lettering objects as well as adjusting
lettering stitch angles.

Applying different stitch types to
lettering objects
By default, lettering objects are filled with Satin
stitch. You can also apply other basic fill stitch
types, such as Step or Fancy, as with other
embroidery objects. See Selecting outline
stitches for details.

tight, thus requiring fewer stitches to cover the
fabric. Where a column is very narrow, stitches
need to be less dense because too many needle
penetrations can damage the fabric.
You can fill lettering shapes with Special Satin
stitching as well as the regular satin stitch. It is
particularly suitable for filling large, irregular
lettering shapes that are too large for satin
stitch. See also Adjusting Satin fill spacing.

Adjusting Satin stitch settings for
lettering objects

stitch. Where a letter is narrow, stitches are

Chapter 22

Improving Stitch Quality

Use automatic underlay and pull compensation to achieve smooth,
even placement of stitches, and eliminate gaps in your design. These
features are object properties, and can be applied, removed or
modified at any time.
This section describes how to strengthen and stabilize designs with
automatic underlays, as well as how to compensate for fabric stretch.
Techniques for controlling long stitches with Auto Jump are described
and you will also find details of setting automatic start and end points.

Strengthening and stabilizing with Applying automatic underlays
underlays
Use Underlay (General toolbar) to strengthen
Underlay stitching helps stabilize fabric and
reduces distortion due to the pull effect. It also
provides ‘loft’, raising cover stitches and
preventing them from sinking into soft fabrics.
When no underlay is applied, the embroidery
lays flat on the fabric. It not only looks flat, but
the underlying fabric often shows through. New
digitizers might be tempted to increase stitch
density but it is more effective to apply a proper
underlay.
Underlay generates underlay stitching for
objects based on the settings specified in the
Effects dialog. Underlay settings are stored with
each object in the same way as other object
properties. Thus they are regenerated
whenever the object is scaled or transformed.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

and stabilize embroidery designs with
automatic underlay.

You can generate automatic underlay stitching
from scratch or apply it to existing objects. The
type of underlay you choose is determined by
the purpose it is to serve.

To apply automatic underlays

 Click the Auto Underlay icon with or without
objects selected.

195

Chapter 22: Improving Stitch Quality
Underlay stitches are automatically
generated for any new objects and all
selected objects. For both new or selected
objects, automatically generated underlay
stitches are based on current properties.

Compensating for fabric stretch
Embroidery stitches pull the fabric inwards
where the needle penetrates. This can cause the
fabric to pucker, and gaps to appear in the
embroidery. Shapes are slightly narrower and
longer on the final embroidery than they look on
the screen. Pull compensation counters this

Preserving long stitches with Auto
Jump
Embroidery machines have a maximum possible
stitch length which is determined by the frame
movement limitations of the machine itself. If a
stitch exceeds this, it is broken into smaller
stitches. This can affect the appearance of the
embroidery, especially Satin fills. By applying
Auto Jump, you can preserve long stitches in an
object by turning them into a series of jumps.

maximum stitch

long stitch is broken into smaller
needle arm movements — jumps

Auto Jump is stored as an object property and is
turned on by default. You can change its values
at any time for selected or newly created
objects in the Object Properties dialog. Auto
Jump values can be adjusted for Fill stitch,
Outline Satin and Outline Blanket stitch.

Applying Auto Jump
You can apply Auto Jump to objects in a design
either before or after you digitize. Apply Auto
Jump to objects with stitches that exceed the
maximum stitch length.
Auto Jump ON

Note Auto Jump is applied by default to
connectors.
Auto Jump can be used, for example, with
manually digitized underlays. It can also be
used to create quilted effects, for example, by
applying it to Satin areas that are over-stitched
with Run stitch or Pattern Fill.
To create a narrow column with more ‘loft’ than
normal Satin, apply Auto Jump, in conjunction
with a suitable underlay, with the maximum
stitch length set to a small value, e.g. 6 mm. If
the cover stitches are short, splitting them with
a jump makes them looser and thus more
effectively raised off the fabric. See
Strengthening and stabilizing with underlays for
details.

Auto Jump OFF

To apply Auto Jump

1 Select and right-click an object.
The Object Properties dialog opens.
Note With no objects selected, Auto Jump is
applied to all new objects. For both new or
selected objects, Auto Jump is based on
current properties.

2 Click Effects.
The Effects dialog opens.

Setting automatic start and end
points
Use Start and End (Arrange menu) to set the start
and end points of the design.
Before stitching, some embroidery machines
require you to position the starting needle
exactly above the first needle penetration point.

Chapter 23

Specialized Digitizing Techniques

BERNINA artista provides specialized digitizing features to save time
as you digitize, and for special effects and machine functions. There
are tools for digitizing appliqué objects, as well as methods for cutting
holes, creating repeated or ‘backtracked’ duplicates, and adding
borders.
This section describes how to reinforce outlines and cut holes in
objects. You can also find out about specialist digitizing for appliqué.

Reinforcing outlines
Use Double Run (Design toolbar) to reinforce
an outline, stitching it in the opposite
direction.
Use the Double Run
feature to reinforce
outlines. Double Run
stitches in reverse
direction to the original. It
is typically used to make
run stitch outlines thicker
without creating
unwanted connecting stitches.

The object is duplicated with the stitching
reversed, and placed on top of the original.
It is the same color as the original and is
positioned after it in the stitching sequence.
Tip Press Ctrl + B to reinforce the object.

3 Check that the object has been duplicated by
using one of the following methods:

 Check the stitch count in the Status Line.
 Use Slow Redraw. See Viewing the
stitching sequence slowly for details.

 Travel through the stitches. See Viewing
the stitching sequence for details.

To reinforce outlines

1 Select the object/s to reinforce an outline.
2 Click the Double Run icon.

maintain the shapes but
remove one layer of stitching. Alternatively you

holes.

Creating buttonholes

The current buttonhole displays as an
outline.

BERNINA artista allows you to insert preset
buttonholes, to specify the size of buttonholes
and merge the buttonhole into a design.
Buttonholes are usually formed with satin
column stitches bordering a slit that is
approximately the length of the button
diameter. The satin stitch border protects the
slit from fraying. The buttonhole consists of a
bead of satin stitches with a bar tack.

Creating fills with Fancy fill
Fancy fill is a decorative fill stitch, similar to
Step fill, in which the needle penetrations form
a tiled pattern. Use Fancy fill to fill wide and
large areas with unique artistic effects while
keeping the appearance of a solid field of
stitching. You can change stitch values to give
greater variation.

Chapter 25

Pattern Runs and Fills

Patterns are pre-defined design elements,
such as hearts, leaves or border patterns,
that can be quickly inserted into a design.
They generally consist of one or more simple
objects, and are stored in a special pattern
set.
You can create your own patterns or use the
ones provided with the software. Patterns
can be scaled, rotated and mirrored in the
same way as other objects. You can use
patterns along a digitized line. You can also
fill shapes with rows of repeated patterns,
and apply special effects, using Pattern Fill.
This section describes how to insert patterns into your design, and how to adjust their
settings to get the results you want. It also explains how to create your own patterns
and pattern sets.

Creating pattern runs

Selecting and inserting pattern runs

Pattern Run is a stitch type that creates pattern
repeats along a digitized line. You can create
decorative outlines using any pattern from the
selection list. You can modify the rotation angle,
orientation and scale, and vary the space
between patterns.

Select the pattern runs
before or after digitizing. If
you use Pattern Run without
selecting a pattern, the
default will be used. See also
Changing pattern runs.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

223

Chapter 25: Pattern Runs and Fills

selected.

Pattern Run from the Stitch Type list.

Chapter 26

Craft Stitch Borders and Fills

BERNINA artista provides a range of craft stitches
which you can use in your designs to mimic some
traditional hand-worked embroidery. These include a
large range of blackwork and candlewicking patterns
suitable for both outlines and fill. See Craft Stitch
Pattern Samples for details.
Backstitch and stemstich are two versatile outline
stitches. Backstitch forms a continuous thin line,
similar to machine stitching, while stemstich produces
rope-like lines which can vary in thickness depending
on the angle of the stitches. Both can be used to make
a smooth continuous row of stitching suitable for
outlines and for blackwork.
Lacework and stipple are two decorative fill stitches.
Lacework can be used to give a lacey look to objects
such as flowers. Stippling, also known as vermicelli, is a free flowing running stitch that
can be used for decorative effects or to produce quilting.
This section explains how to select craft stitch types, as well as change stitch settings to
obtain the best results.

Creating craft stitch outlines
Click Outline (Stitch & Color toolbar) for outline
stitches.
Use Blackwork Border (Craft Stitch toolbar) to
outline blackwork designs.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Use Candlewicking Border (Craft Stitch
toolbar) for candlewick borders.
Use Backstitch (Craft Stitch toolbar) for
backstitch borders.
Use Stemstitch (Craft Stitch toolbar) for
stemstitch borders.

233

Chapter 26: Craft Stitch Borders and Fills
Different stitch types suit different shapes.
When you digitize an object, it uses the current
stitch type for the selected digitizing method.
However, you can change stitch types at any
stage. See Setting current object properties for
details.

outlines

Blackwork gets its

silk thread traditionally
used in this form of
embroidery. Early
designs tend to be
angular, whereas later
designs use more
diagonal stitches.

It can be used to decorate articles of dress or
furnishings such as hankies, table napkins,
tablecloths, and doilies.

Digitizing for Appliqué

Automatically create all the stitching you need for
appliqué using Auto Appliqué or you can create the
appliqué manually. When you digitize an object with Auto
Appliqué, the placement line, cutting line, tackdown and
cover stitches are automatically generated using the
current Auto Appliqué settings.

Creating appliqué objects
Use Auto Appliqué to produce the stitching you
require for appliqué objects. The cover stitch is
the border around the appliqué shape. You can
control various settings including cover stitch
type, width, stitch spacing, as well as the offset.
Tip You can also create appliqué objects with
partial cover stitching to create an overlapping
effect without doubling-up borders. See also
Creating partial cover appliqué objects.

Creating appliqué objects using Auto
Appliqué
Use Auto Appliqué (Design toolbar) to digitize
appliqué objects.
Use Auto Appliqué to produce the stitching you
require for appliqué objects. Up to four layers of
stitching — placement line, cutting line,
tackdown and cover stitch — are generated for
each appliqué object according to the current
settings.

To create appliqué objects using Auto
Appliqué

1 Load a picture in Picture mode.
2 Click Design mode.

Chapter 28

Processing Files of Different Formats

By default BERNINA artista saves designs to its native
file format, ART. This format contains all information
necessary both for stitching a design and for later
modification. When opening designs created or saved in
other formats, BERNINA artista converts the design
information so you can modify it using the full range of
BERNINA artista features. Depending on the file type,
you may need to provide additional information to
assist the conversion process.
This section describes how to select conversion options
for opening and saving different stitch and outline
design formats.

Embroidery design formats
Embroidery designs are saved in one of three
formats — pure ART format, ‘outline’ format or
‘stitch’ format.
Note For details of specific formats supported
by BERNINA artista, see Supported embroidery
file formats and Comparison of outline and
stitch format information.

ART files
Native ART designs, called ‘ART Grade A’,
contain a complete set of design information in
a single file – object outlines and properties,

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

actual stitches, thread colors, a picture icon and
comments. Thus only native ART files provide
100% perfect scaling and transformation.
There are four grades of ART file, depending on
how the ART file was created, Grade A is the
best format to use, Grade B is more reliable
than Grade C, but not as good as Grade A
format, and Grade D is the least reliable.
File source

Description

Art Grade A

Pure ART file created in BERNINA
artista based software. They contain
pure ART objects, outlines and
stitches.

253

Chapter 28: Processing Files of Different Formats
artista, corresponding stitch types, input
methods and effects are applied.

File source

Description

Art Grade B

Designs read from outline format
such as CND and saved as ART
files. You cannot create the Grade B
designs in BERNINA artista, but
once converted to ART in other
software, BERNINA artista reads
these as Grade B designs.

Art Grade C

Designs read from stitch files —
SEW, PCS, PES, etc — where
stitches have been converted to
objects. See Opening stitch files
with object conversion for details.

Art Grade D

Designs read from stitch files —
SEW, PCS, PES, etc — where
stitches have NOT been converted
to outlines. See Opening stitch files
without object conversion for details.

Outline files can be scaled, transformed and
reshaped without affecting stitch density or
quality. After modification, you can save your
design to the native ART format or to a different
format altogether.
To scale and transform (mirror, skew, rotate)
designs and regenerate stitches, BERNINA
artista requires design outline information.
Therefore full design transformation and
generation options are only available for outline
format files.

For information about the source of a design file,
refer to the Design information in the Open
dialog, or in the Design Properties dialog. See
Viewing design information in BERNINA ARTlink
for details.

Native ART design

Stitch files
Different embroidery machines understand
different languages. Each has its own control
commands for the various machine functions.
Before you can stitch a design, it must be in a
format which can be interpreted by the
machine.
Native ART design

Outline files
Outline or ‘condensed’ files, such as ART, are
high-level formats which contain object
outlines, object properties and stitch data.
When you open an outline file in BERNINA

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Stitch or ‘expanded’ designs are low-level
formats for direct use by embroidery machines.
They contain only stitch coordinates and
machine functions. They are generally created
‘on the fly’ when sending designs to machine.
They can also be output to embroidery disk or
design card.
When they are read into BERNINA artista, stitch
files do not contain object information such as
outlines or stitch types, but present the design

254

Chapter 28: Processing Files of Different Formats
as a collection of stitch blocks. Stitch blocks are
created wherever color changes or trims are
detected in the design. They have only General
properties.
Stitch designs are generally not suited to
modification because stitches are not
regenerated. You will be warned if the design
you are opening should not be scaled.

scaled stitch
design

Tip You can make this warning display when
opening designs by selecting Help > Show
Design Warning.
BERNINA artista can, however, interpret object
outlines, stitch types and spacing from stitch
data with some success. These ‘converted’
designs can be scaled and stitches recalculated.
Note Processing is effective for most stitch
designs but cannot produce the same level of
quality as original outlines and may not handle
some fancy stitches.
By default, stitch files are converted to outlines
and objects upon opening but you can also
choose to open them without conversion.
Designs opened in this way can be output for
stitching in another format. Or you can edit
stitches and add new elements.
Note You can scale stitch format designs which
have been opened without object conversion.
However, because the stitch count does not
change, the density increases or decreases with
the design size. Thus you should not scale stitch
designs by more than ±5% or some areas may
be too thickly or too thinly covered.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Note With or without object conversion, stitch
files can be saved in ART format once they are
opened in BERNINA artista.

Opening stitch files in BERNINA
artista
If you want to scale a design, edit an outline or
change a stitch type, then the design must be
converted into objects. By default, stitch files
are converted upon opening.
If you do not want to scale the design by more
than ±5%, or only want to make minor stitch
edits, or output in another format, you can
choose to open the stitch file without object
conversion.
Tip To ensure object outlines, stitch types,
stitch density and colors are correct, it is always
a good idea to check and edit designs after
conversion.

Opening stitch files with object
conversion
When you scale a design, you change the
number of stitches needed to fill the shape.
BERNINA artista re-calculates and redraws all

255

Chapter 28: Processing Files of Different Formats
stitches automatically. This is called ‘stitch
processing’, and it ensures that the design you
see on-screen appears as it will be stitched.
When you open stitch files with object
conversion, the design is converted to a Grade
C ART file.
There are times, however, when you want to
switch off stitch processing. For example, you
may have used special effects or made manual
edits to stitches, which will be lost when stitch
processing occurs as you scale the design. You
can switch off stitch processing for each design
as you open it.
Note This option applies only to stitch files,
such as PES files — it does not apply to ART
files.

The Open Options dialog opens.

Stitches Into
Object Shapes

converted to objects with fill or outline stitch
properties as well as general properties. If an
object is not recognized, it becomes an
object with general property only.

Chapter 29

Outputting and Storing Designs

You can output embroidery designs in a variety of ways —
by sending directly to machine for stitching or saving to
your machine, a design card or PC.
When you send designs directly to the machine for
stitching, they will be lost when the machine is turned off.
To keep designs, write them to the machine’s embroidery
module memory or a design card; or save them to your
PC’s hard disk, CD or a floppy disk. Saved designs can
then be retrieved and edited in BERNINA artista. You can
also delete or rename saved designs.
This section describes how to check and print design
information, and how to generate different outputs from
your designs as well as how to read and write embroidery
design files to design cards.

Previewing and printing designs
You can create a hard copy of your designs using
default or custom printer options. You can also
preview designs before printing. Set print
options to display the information you want. If
you are using a color printer, you can print in
Artistic View.

Setting print options
You can print a copy of your design with
information about the design size, color
sequence, and stitch count, together with an

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

image of the design. Print a copy of the appliqué
pattern for cutting out the fabric pieces.

1 Access the Print dialog. Either:
 Select File > Print.

Chapter 30

Magic Box

Magic Box allows you to convert between designs saved
in a variety of ‘non-native’ formats. You can read a
design from any supported design card into BERNINA
artista, edit it then write it to a Magic Card. If you obtain
a design from a Magic Card or other design card, you can
only write it back to a Magic Card. You cannot save it to
your computer or write it to a design card other than
Magic Card.
The Magic Box software is installed automatically when
you install BERNINA artista and only works with
BERNINA artista. This section deals with how to use the Magic Box design card
conversion software. The instructions included here only apply to the operation of the
Magic Box with BERNINA artista. Refer to the Magic Box User Manual for installation,
setup and operating instructions.
Note too that the instructions cover the use of the serial port Magic Box. For the USB
Magic Box see the Bernina/OESD Online Support Center at:
http://berninasupport.custhelp.com/cgi-bin/berninasupport.cfg/php/enduser/home.php

If you are using a USB Magic Box, you will need to update your BERNINA artista software
so that it is using the correct version software. Refer to the latest BERNINA artista
Support Notes for more details.

Using Magic Box
From BERNINA artista you can read designs
from CD, floppy disk, or hard drive. You can also
read designs from Magic Box in any supported
design card format. From there you can write to
Magic Box using Magic Cards of any of four
types. Use the Magic Card which supports the

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

format you need. See Supported design card
formats for details.
Note You cannot write designs read from Magic
Box to your computer floppy disk or hard drive.
Nor can you write to design cards other than
Magic Cards.

Chapter 31

Hardware Setup and System Settings

You will need to connect peripheral devices for use with BERNINA
artista. These may include printers, scanners and embroidery
machines.
BERNINA artista lets you adjust various system settings
controlling the appearance of designs on screen, display of design
information, the behavior of the design window, and other
settings.
This section describes how to set up embroidery machines, and
scanners and describes how to calibrate the monitor. It also
describes how to change grid spacing and positioning options, and
change hoop options. There is also information about how to adjust the pointer position
display, and how to set automatic save.

Setting up hardware
Different devices are set up in different ways —
some in Windows, via the Control Panel, others
within BERNINA artista itself. For instructions on
connecting devices to your computer and
setting up in Windows, see the documentation
for the device as well as your Microsoft Windows
documentation.

Peripheral device connection settings
Peripheral devices such as printers are
connected to an available ‘port’ at the front or
back of your computer. These may be of either
‘serial’, ‘parallel’ or USB type. The software

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

settings need to be configured within the
Windows Control Panel.

Serial port settings
PC serial COM ports are male connectors, and
can be either 9-pin or 25-pin. They are named
COM1, COM2, etc. The number of available
ports limits the number of devices you can
connect. If additional ports are required, you
can add them. BERNINA artista allows up to 4
serial ports.
Note You must be able to identify the ports on
your computer. If in doubt, consult a PC
technician.

275

Chapter 31: Hardware Setup and System Settings

Parallel port settings
Parallel ports are used to connect your printer
and scanner. They are named LPT1, LPT2, etc.

USB port settings
USB — Universal Serial Bus — ports are used to
connect your dongle. They can also be used to
connect your printer or scanner. Additional USB
ports can use a powered USB hub.

Setting up embroidery machines
Your BERNINA home embroidery machine
should be connected to the COM1, COM2,
COM3, COM4 or USB port of your computer.
See your BERNINA home embroidery machine
manual for details on connecting the machine to
your computer.

Setting up printers
Your printer is connected to a USB or parallel
port. Some printers can be connected to either
port type. BERNINA artista will use the default
printer that you have specified through the
Printer icon in the Windows Control Panel.
See your printer manual for directions on
setting it up properly. See Windows Help (select
Windows Start > Help > Index, then type
‘default printer’) for directions on how to set up
a default printer.

Note Some scanners will not work with
BERNINA artista because they require their own
scanner software. If this is the case with your
scanner, use your scanner software for
scanning, save the image to your hard drive,
then load the image into your BERNINA artista
program.

To set up scanners

1 Connect the scanner using the
accompanying instructions.

2 Set it up in Windows using the accompanying
instructions and/or the Microsoft Windows
documentation.

3 Start BERNINA artista.
4 Select Settings > Scanner Setup.
The Select Source dialog opens displaying a
list of scanner drivers loaded on your
computer.

Select a
scanning driver

5 Select the scanning driver to use, then click
Select.

Setting up scanners
Use Scanner Setup (Settings menu) to setup
scanners.
BERNINA artista supports TWAIN-compatible
scanners.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Note If you have trouble with scanning after
re-starting BERNINA artista, there may be a
conflict with previously installed scanner
drivers. Re-install BERNINA artista and test
the scanner. If the selected scanner driver
does not work in BERNINA artista, select
another scanner driver from the list. There
are usually two installed for each scanner.

276

Chapter 31: Hardware Setup and System Settings

monitor.
You need to calibrate your monitor so that
designs at 1:1 scale appear at real size. Do this
when you first install BERNINA artista or
whenever you change your monitor.

To calibrate your monitor

1 Select Settings > Display Setup.
The Screen Calibration dialog opens.

by setting a reference point — for example,
when you want grid lines to intersect the center
point of the design, or a horizontal grid line to
sit under a particular object.
Use Snap to Grid to align objects in your design
to the nearest grid lines. You can align objects
to the horizontal (X) or vertical (Y) axis, or both.
New reference points will ‘snap’ to the nearest
grid line as you digitize. Existing objects will
snap to grid when you select and move them.

Chapter 31: Hardware Setup and System Settings
saving of designs, the position of the pointer

Setting automatic save
Select Options (Settings menu) to set automatic
save and backup options
Save your work automatically at regular
intervals using Auto Save to protect you from
losing work in the event of hardware or software
failure.
A backup file is created every time you save a
design. The design will be saved in the BACKUP
folder of your BERNINA artista installation. It
will have the same name as the original file with
the extension BAK.
Caution Backup files remain in the BACKUP
folder until you delete them. To prevent the
folder from using too much hard disk space,
delete unwanted files regularly.

To set automatic save

1 Select Settings > Options > General tab.

Interpreting pointer position values
The X and Y values show the horizontal and
vertical distance of the pointer from the relative
or absolute point. The L value is the length of
the line connecting the pointer with the previous
point, while A is the angle of that line, relative
to the horizontal.
1
length = 60.6 mm
2

Y = -32.8 mm
X = -50.9 mm
Y: vertical

Status Bar

X: horizontal

A: angle

L: length

A negative X value indicates that the second
point was placed to the left of the first point,
while a negative Y value displays when the
second point is below the first. Negative angles
indicate angles of more than 180º — for
example, an angle of -60º is equal to 300º.

Chapter 32

Portfolio Basics

BERNINA artista Portfolio provides an efficient way
for viewing and managing embroidery designs. This
design management tool can access design files
stored on your computer hard disk, CD-ROM, or
floppy disk. Portfolio even lets you archive and view
design files in folders which have been archived by
means of the popular WinZip utility. It recognizes
all design file formats used by BERNINA artista. See
Converting design files in folders for details.
This section describes the complete workings of
BERNINA artista Portfolio from start up to viewing,
sorting, and converting designs. It also explains
how to use BERNINA artista Portfolio to publish
design catalogs.

Opening Portfolio and setting
defaults
BERNINA artista Portfolio does not require a
separate installation. It works as an integral
part of BERNINA artista and is easy to set up
and get started.

Opening Portfolio

Note If you start Portfolio from the desktop or
from the Windows Start button, BERNINA artista
will also open. If your computer is slow or you
have many applications running, Portfolio may
not open.
Tip Close Portfolio in View by Details mode so
that it opens more quickly next time.

Portfolio may be opened from the desktop or
from the Windows Start button.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

283

Chapter 32: Portfolio Basics

that the design is missing. See also Locating

Locating missing files
If users incorrectly move files, the normal
design thumbnail is usually replaced by a
‘missing design’ thumbnail when Portfolio is
next opened. Each color indicates the likely
cause:
Icon

Description
This means that Portfolio cannot find
the design file. Replace missing files
from backup or another source. If this is
not possible, delete the record or
refresh the directory. See also
Refreshing the display.
This means that Portfolio has found an
up-to-date thumbnail but doesn’t
recognize the format. Use Validate and
Refresh to update the display. Portfolio
will try to make a replacement next time
the folder is opened or refreshed. See
also Refreshing the display.
Portfolio recognizes a pre-determined
set of file types as per the file type
dropdown list. If it sees a design that
has the required file suffix — ART,
BMP, etc — but it is unable to read the
file, it will display the green question
mark. Locate the file and delete it.

Portfolio Advanced

Designs in BERNINA artista Portfolio folders can be
sorted in three main ways. This is useful when you
want to select designs for stitching out, printing,
archiving, and so on. Once a design is selected in
an Portfolio folder, you can send it direct to an
embroidery machine. You can also convert your
ART and other design files to and from other file
formats directly from Portfolio.
Catalogs of designs in a folder contain thumbnails
and/or summary information. These can be sent to
a printer and published in a variety of formats.
Portfolio lets you archive and view design files in
folders which have been archived by means of the
popular WinZip utility. This can be useful for sending or receiving multiple files via email.
This section describes the more advanced workings of Portfolio from sorting files to
stitching, and converting designs. It also describes how to print and publish catalogs of
designs, as well as how to archive dormant or unused designs.

Browsing designs in folders
Designs in folders can be browsed using
navigation buttons and slide show buttons on
the Properties dialog. You can do this to both
the whole folder or to designs selected using
Search or Sort. You can also filter the contents
of any design folder to show only certain types
of file — e.g. only ART files. See Sorting files and
Limiting file types displayed for details.

Part IX

Cross Stitch

306

Chapter 34

Introduction to Cross Stitch

Cross stitching is a popular technique for filling large areas with
low stitch counts. It can also be used for outlines and borders.
It is suitable for homeware, tablecloths, children’s clothes and
folk designs. Cross stitch is sometimes combined with appliqué.
Unlike machine embroidery where designs are enhanced with
different stitch types, angles, and effects, in cross stitch you
can only add dimension to the design with shades of colors. For
this reason, most cross stitch designs use many more colors
than machine embroidery.
This section provides an overview of the various cross stitch
types.

Types of cross stitch
Cross stitches are created on a matrix of
squares or ‘pixels’. Any part of the square can
be stitched, from the edges to the diagonals.
The full range of cross stitches is listed below.

Cross stitch fills
When using cross stitch as a fill you can select
from any of the cross stitch fill types.

Full cross stitch

Note The long arm of the three-quarter stitch
can go either / or \ directions.
Frequently a quarter stitch and a three-quarter
stitch of different colors share a single square.
Mix and match their positions to give different
effects.

Full cross stitches are made up of two
equal-length stitches that cross at the center to
form an X. The overall effect of the fill depends
on which stitch in the X lies underneath or on
top. You can also mix and match for special
effects.

full cross stitch
pattern

mixtures of quarter and a three-quarter stitches

Half cross stitch

Three-quarter cross stitch
A three-quarter stitch is most often created by
stitching a quarter stitch followed by a half
stitch. A different effect is achieved by stitching
the half stitch first and anchoring it with the
quarter stitch.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

A half stitch is simply a diagonal stitch that looks
like / or like \. Half stitches are usually used for
shadows and background shading. The stitch
can start from any corner. Sometimes two half
stitches of different colors are used to make one
full cross stitch.

half cross stitch
pattern

308

Chapter 34: Introduction to Cross Stitch
Half cross can go in either direction depending
on the effect you want to achieve. For example,
half cross used to represent feathers would
slant in the direction of the feathers themselves.

upright cross stitch
pattern

Quarter cross stitch
Quarter stitches are used to stitch more
intricate patterns.
quarter cross stitch
pattern

Double cross stitch
Double cross stitch is two full cross stitches
stitched at 90º on top of each other. A double
cross stitch resembles a star. It is generally used
in a scattered fashion or worked into a row to
form a decorative border.
double cross stitch
pattern

Mini cross stitch
Mini cross stitches are two equal-length stitches
that cross at the center to form an X but they
only fill quarter of the square. Use mini cross
stitch for details such as eyes or for a denser
look in parts of your design.
mini cross stitch
pattern

Typically the top thread of the bottom cross is
the stitch which lies at 135º to the horizontal;
and the top thread of the overlapping cross is
the horizontal stitch.

Elongated cross stitch

Upright cross stitch
Upright cross stitches are comprised of a
vertical and horizontal stitch.
The stitches can be worked diagonally across
the canvas, or in a horizontal or vertical line.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Elongated cross stitch consists of two
equal-length stitches that cross to form an X.
There are two types:

 elongated horizontal cross stitch
 elongated vertical cross stitch.
Elongated horizontal cross stitches are only half
the height of normal cross stitch and elongated
vertical cross stitches are only half the width of
normal cross stitch.

309

Chapter 34: Introduction to Cross Stitch

elongated cross
stitch pattern

the software as a border while a Full Cross
outline is treated as a fill. This is important if you
need to delete a border because a single line can
be deleted as a single object while a full cross
stitch border cannot.

Single line border

French knots
French knots were originally a hand-stitch used
for a raised look for embellishment in Europe. It
was traditionally used in ‘whitework’ with very
exclusive cotton, and fine silk threads. French
knots can be used in combination with
cross-stitch or outline stitch.

Single Line stitches are only used to create
borders or add details. The single line can be
placed on any side of the square. Click on the
side you want to place the line. You can place a
single line border on one side or all four sides of
the square. The single line border can be placed
in a square which already has a cross stitch in it.

single line border

french knots cross
stitch pattern

Full cross border

Use french knots for eyes, door knobs, dots for
flower pistols or in the place of beads used in
hand-work designs, e.g. ornaments on a
Christmas tree, to dot the letter i. Use it as an
outline border. Try it with colorful metallic or
variegated threads. French knots are better
sewn in larger grids.

Cross stitch borders

Full crosses can also be used as a decorative
outline stitch. If you use full cross as a border it
will be the same cross stitch type as selected in
the Fill stitch type list. You can change this by
changing the Fill stitch type.

full cross border

Cross stitch can be used as an embroidery
outline or border. You can also combine cross
stitching with other stitch types to achieve
special effects.

Chapter 35

Cross Stitch Essentials

In BERNINA artista Cross Stitch you can
add cross stitches either to an entire
design or selected areas. Or you can
place them individually. There are two
stitch types, outline and fill. An outline
stitch can be a single run, or a line of
cross stitches. A fill can be any of the
cross stitch types. You can pre-set stitch
type, length and the number of times each stitch is sewn.
This section describes the use of the cross stitch design window, techniques for manual
cross stitch digitizing, and cross stitch design file handling.

Opening Cross Stitch

The Cross Stitch design window opens.

Click Cross Stitch (Design toolbar) to launch
the Cross Stitch application.
Cross stitch design involves a different digitizing
technique to ordinary embroidery design. For
this reason, the cross stitch application has its
own design window which you open from within
BERNINA artista.

To open Cross Stitch

1 Run BERNINA artista.
2 Select Design mode.
3 Click the Cross Stitch icon.

Setting design size and density
In BERNINA artista Cross Stitch, design size is
controlled by the ‘fabric count’. The more
stitches per inch, for example, the smaller and
more compact the resulting design. You can also
adjust the density of individual cross stitches by
stitching them once, twice or three times.

Setting the design size

stitches shown as crosses

cross stitches shown as
colored squares

You set cross stitch design size by adjusting the
‘fabric count’. This actually changes the scale of
your finished design. The more stitches per
inch, for example, the smaller and more
compact the resulting design.
Caution A common problem with cross stitch
designs is that they ‘pull’, especially with
smaller, closely packed stitches. Cross stitch
designs tend to pull in a uniform way with gaps
usually forming in straight lines. Sometimes
holes appear giving the impression of missing
stitches. See also Setting the stitch density.

stitches shown as crosses

fractional stitches shown as
colored squares

1 Select Settings > Fabric Count.
The Fabric Count dialog opens.

well as the zoom factor of the design.

Setting the stitch density
Use the Threads list on the
Stitch / Color bar to set the
stitch density.
You can adjust the density of any cross stitch by
stitching it once, twice or three times. The
number of ‘crossovers’ correspond to the values
2, 4 and 6 in the Threads list. You can change
these values at any time.

stitches to create different patterning effects.

Chapter 36

Cross Stitch Editing

BERNINA artista Cross Stitch provides techniques for
editing cross stitch designs, including moving, rotating, and
mirroring stitch blocks. You can erase stitches as well as
change colors and stitch types of selected areas. You can
clone a selection for re-use as a ‘rubber stamp’ and fill large
areas with motifs or special composite stitches. You can also
edit stitches to create combinations of partial crosses
comprised of multiple colors.
This section provides details of cross stitch editing features.

Selecting and locking stitches
In order to edit an area of a design, you must
first select it. BERNINA artista Cross Stitch
provides a number of ways to select cross
stitches. It also lets you lock cross stitches
and/or outlines so that they are not moved or
edited by accident.

Selecting cross stitches with Select
Object or Polygon Select
Use Select Object (Design toolbar) to select
a single stitch or block of stitches.
Use Polygon Select (Design toolbar) to select
irregular areas or shapes.
The Select Object and Polygon Select tools work
in a similar way to BERNINA artista. The
difference is that in BERNINA artista Cross
Stitch you work with stitch blocks or areas
rather than design objects.
Caution You cannot select cross stitches
outside the hoop.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

327

Chapter 36: Cross Stitch Editing

To select cross stitches with Select Object or
Polygon Select

 With the Select Object tool selected, simply
drag a bounding box around the area to be
selected.

 With the Polygon Select tool selected, mark
reference points around the area you want to
select and press Enter.

or a closed undigitized area. Only adjoining
stitches of the same color are selected.

Chapter 37

Advanced Cross Stitching

The BERNINA artista Cross Stitch design window
also lets you import pictures to use as backdrops
or for automatic digitizing. You can also create
special cross stitch lettering.
This section provides details of advanced cross
stitch features including automatic digitizing and
lettering.

Digitizing with backdrops
Bitmap images can be inserted or scanned into
BERNINA artista Cross Stitch for use as
digitizing backdrops. You can digitize backdrop
images automatically using Auto Stitch. Use
solid color bitmaps to make suitable color blocks
for cross stitches.
Tip If an image needs to be resized or rotated,
it is best to do so during scanning. Scaling
afterwards may distort the image.

Inserting bitmap images
Use Load Picture (Picture toolbar) to open a
picture.
Use Scan (Picture toolbar) to scan your
artwork.
You can insert bitmap images of various formats
for use as digitizing backdrops. See Supported
drawing and image formats in the User Manual.

To insert images

1 Click the New icon.
A new file opens.

2 Click Picture mode to open a picture.
3 Click Load Picture.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

335

Chapter 37: Advanced Cross Stitching
The Open Design dialog opens.

4 Select a folder from the Look In list.
5 Select a file type from the Files of Type list
(e.g. BMP).

6 Select the file you want to insert.
Tip Select the Preview checkbox to preview
the selected file.

7 Click Open.
8 Move the picture so it fits inside the hoop or
change the hoop size. See Changing hoop
settings for details.
Note Areas outside the hoop will not be
stitched.

Viewing or hiding bitmap images
Click Picture (Standard toolbar) to show or
hide the picture.
You can show or hide a bitmap backdrop
temporarily while you digitize in Design mode.

show picture

hide picture

Scaling and transforming bitmap images
You can scale and reshape images once they are
loaded into BERNINA artista Cross Stitch.
However, if an image needs to be resized or
rotated, it is best to do so during scanning.
Scaling afterwards may distort the image. See
Arranging and Transforming Objects in the User
Manual.

To scale and transform bitmap images

1 Insert the image.
See Inserting bitmap images for details.

2 Click the Select icon.
3 Click the image.
Eight resize selection handles appear around
the image. Click and drag these to scale the
image as you would any other object. See
Scaling objects in the User Manual.

To view or hide bitmap images

 Click the Picture icon to show the image.
 Click it again to hide the image.

Use Lettering (General toolbar) to add letters
to your design. Right-click Lettering to open
the Font dialog.
As with BERNINA artista, Cross Stitch lets you
add lettering to a design by typing it directly in
the design window. In Cross Stitch use the Font
dialog to select font, size and style. Select either
outline or fill to digitize letters. Once typed, you
cannot edit letters. Each letter is treated as a
stitch block.

Appendix E

Supported File Formats

The following tables show how various features
are converted when the file type is changed. Use
this section when converting designs to different
formats, or digitizing for a specific format other
than ART.
Other format designs, which can be read into
BERNINA artista, are stitch-based designs. This
means that they were not created object by
object, but rather stitch by stitch.
When you open another format design into
BERNINA artista, the software reads the data
and determines the object outlines and the
object properties so that you can stitch and
customize these designs like other BERNINA
artista designs.
You can also use Magic Box to read and write
designs in other formats. See Magic Box for
details.
This chapter describes the recommendations for
working with other format designs, and the
limitations.
Note This BERNINA embroidery software
product will convert designs to other non-native
home formats which are current at the time of
development. Later releases of other brand
software may not produce compatible formats
of the same file types.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Supported embroidery file
formats
There are three types of embroidery file
formats:

 Outline files: Outline or ‘condensed’ files

usually contain digitized shapes and lines,
selected stitch types and stitch values and
effects.
 Stitch files: Stitch or ‘expanded’ files contain
only stitches and machine functions and are
suited to specific embroidery machines.
See Processing Files of Different Formats for
details.
 ART format files:
See the description below for the different
types of ART file.

ART format files
There are grades of ART file, depending on how
the ART file was created, Grade A is the best
format to use, Grade B is more reliable than
Grade C, but not as good as Grade A format, and
Grade D is the least reliable.

 Grade A: Pure ART file created in BERNINA
artista based software.
 Grade B: Designs read from outline format
such as CND and saved as ART files. You
cannot create the Grade B designs in

365

Appendix E: Supported File Formats
BERNINA artista, but once converted to ART
in other software, BERNINA artista can read
these as Grade B designs.
 Grade C: Designs read from stitch files with
stitch processing and saved as ART files.
 Grade D: Designs read from stitch files
without stitch processing and saved as ART
files.
When an ART design is created by opening a
stitch file with stitch processing, the original
stitches are maintained until you change them.
You can guarantee that the design will stitch as
originally made. However, if you scale the
design or do anything which force the stitches to
be recalculated from new outlines, then stitches
are calculated from the estimated outlines
causing significant differences from the original
stitches.

Grade C

Appendix F

Troubleshooting

This appendix provides help for solving problems in BERNINA
artista. It includes procedures for checking your system’s
requirements and settings, reverting to original values, and testing
disks and connections. It also describes how to log errors, and lists
causes for common error messages and problems.

Solving problems in BERNINA
artista
If you encounter a problem, refer to the
following references for help:

 BERNINA artista User Manual — select

Help > Online Manual (or printed version)
 BERNINA artista Online Help — select
Help > Help Topics
 Windows Online Help — select Start > Help

Security device messages and General
problems sections in this chapter.

If you have made changes to the default
settings in your software and you want to revert
back to the factory settings, use the Revert
utility.

 Windows manual
 Documentation supplied with your hardware. To revert to factory settings
1 Exit BERNINA artista.
If you are unable to solve a problem, you should 2 On the Windows taskbar, click the Start

contact your dealer.

the system requirements, and check the

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

button and select Programs > BERNINA
artista > Revert.
The Revert to Factory Settings dialog opens.

Recovering from hardware or
software failure
If your software crashes due to a hardware or
software failure, recovery files are usually
created. When you restart BERNINA artista after
such a failure, the design you were last working
on is opened automatically or, if Auto Save was
on, the backup file is retrieved. If a recovery file
is opened, save the file to a new name with the
ART extension.

To check your hard disk space

1 Double-click the My Computer icon on your

Note Do not save to the Backup folder or it will
be deleted.

Windows Desktop.

Problem

The system fails and displays
‘unrecoverable’ error.

Cause

The design you tried to open is
corrupt.

Suggestion

C:\artista\Recover folder using the
Purge Recovery utility, or manually
via Windows Explorer, then restart
BERNINA artista. You can also delete
files using Windows Explorer. See
Deleting files with Delete Recovery for
details.

4

You can delete corrupt files using the Delete
Recovery utility. Delete Recovery deletes files in
the C:\artista\Recover and C:\artista\Backup
folders. You should also periodically delete the
backup files created by Auto Save.

Change the BAK extension to ART.
Start BERNINA artista.

Message

Invalid Access Code

Cause

• The access codes you are trying to
enter are incorrect for your security
device.
• You are missing a set of codes.

Suggestion

Check the ID code on the access

Security device not found (Dongle)

Security device messages
This section describes the messages related to
the security device. Most security device
messages are caused by incorrect connection,
access codes, interference or conflict from
another hardware device on the PC.
To prevent security device errors, enter all
access codes as soon as you receive them. If
you skip any access codes, features may
become unavailable, or BERNINA artista may
stop working altogether.
Tip After entering new codes, exit BERNINA
artista and then restart. See Entering security
codes for details.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Message

Security device not found

Cause

Dongle conflicts with other
peripherals on the same parallel port,
dongle cannot be found by artista
software, or dongle damaged.

Suggestion

Remove any other device that is
attached to the same port as the
dongle. If the dongle is then found by
BERNINA artista, there is a conflict
between the devices.
Install a second parallel port. The
dongle can be attached to either port.
Try a different combination of devices
if you have 3 or more devices and
only 2 parallel ports.
If the dongle can still not be found,
reinstall the dongle driver. See
Installing the dongle driver for details.
Uninstall and reinstall your BERNINA
artista software if the above
suggestions do not work.

373

Appendix F: Troubleshooting

Installing the dongle driver

Security Error

If your system cannot locate the dongle
connection, you may need to reinstall the
dongle driver.

Message

Security Device Not Found (-3)

Cause

The hardware or security device is
faulty, or the security device is not
correctly plugged in.

Suggestion

Check the installation of the security
device to ensure it is fitted correctly.
Try using another port and restarting
the software. If this does not work
contact your distributor with your
Dongle Serial Number.

To install the dongle driver

1 From the Windows taskbar, click the Start
button and select Run.
The Run dialog opens.

2 In the Open field, enter:
C:\artista\BIN\HINSTALL /I /PORTMODE=AUTO

Note You must leave a space after both the
L and I.

3 Click OK.
A message informs you that the dongle
driver was installed successfully.

General problems
The following section refers to general problems
you may experience with your designs.

4 Restart your PC.

Design dimensions

Invalid product

Problem

Dimensions of design are incorrect.

Cause

If the design’s dimensions are too
large or too small, the system units of
measurement may be set incorrectly.
The units of measurement are set in
Windows Regional settings.

Suggestion

Change the units of measurement in
Windows. Select Start > Settings >
Control Panel > Regional Settings.

Message

Invalid product

Cause

The security device cannot be found,
or is damaged.

Suggestion

Check the installation of the security
device to ensure it is fitted correctly.
Contact your distributor with your
Dongle Serial Number.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

374

Appendix F: Troubleshooting

Inconsistent output results
Problem

Problems when sending designs to
embroidery machines for stitching.
Inconsistent reading and writing.

Cause

Serial or USB communications
problems. Inconsistent reading and
writing may be cause by wrong port
settings in the operating system.
Other possible causes are that
machine and software versions may
not be compatible. In this case,
contact your dealer.

Suggestion

Change the port settings.

Missing toolbar buttons
Problem

Buttons missing from toolbars or
bottom of toolbars.

Cause

Screen resolution is set too low.
Virus in computer.

Suggestion

Change the screen resolution to
800x600 or higher.
Run your virus checker.

Appendix F: Troubleshooting

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

376

Appendix G

Alphabet Samples

The embroidery alphabets shown in this appendix are a part of your BERNINA artista
program. For the best results when stitching, do not exceed the maximum or minimum
sizes recommended for each of the individual alphabets. For actual embroidery
alphabets and instructions on how to use them, see Adding lettering to embroidery
designs.
For best results, always check that the correct type of underlay is selected. Small,
narrow letters may not require automatic underlay, depending on their size and the
fabric to be stitched on. For example, Anniversary is a very small letter which will stitch
at a minimum of 5mm. If auto underlay is used, it will show outside the stitched
columns. Therefore underlay should be turned off. See also Switching off auto underlay
for small lettering.
A SpecialAlphabet template for manually stitched alphabets — Creative Cross and
Quilting Text — has been included.

Appendix K

Craft Stitch Pattern Samples

The Craft Stitch patterns shown in this appendix are a sample of those included with your
BERNINA artista program. (The online manual contains the full range of Craft Stitch
patterns. You can access it from your BERNINA artista Help menu.) For actual Craft
Stitches and instructions on how to use them, see Craft Stitch Borders and Fills.

Blackwork Fill patterns
Blackwork gets its name from the black silk
thread traditionally used in this form of
embroidery. Blackwork patterns can be used
either as borders or as fills. The most common
blackwork fills employ simple stitches to create
complex scrolling or geometric patterns. See
Adjusting Blackwork Border outlines and
Adjusting Blackwork Fills.

Half Knot

Lacework patterns
Lacework stitch consists of an open square
trellis-like pattern which gives objects a lacey
look. Lacework can only be applied to closed
curve or rectangular filled objects with a single
stitch angle. See Adjusting Lacework fills for
details.
ID

Candlewicking patterns
Candlewicking is a traditional white-on-white
embroidery technique, usually done on white
linen or cotton fabric with heavy cotton threads.
Candlewicking patterns can be used either as
borders or as fills. See Adjusting Candlewicking
Border outlines and Adjusting Candlewicking
Fills.

Appendix L

Quilting Samples

The quilting samples shown in this appendix are a sample of those included with your
BERNINA artista program. (The online manual contains the full range of quilting
samples. You can access it from your BERNINA artista Help menu.) The actual designs
(ART files) can be found in the My Designs\Quilting folder. Files that start with B — e.g.
Block-9 Patch Square — are finished blocks. Files that start with ‘S’ — e.g. Shape-Fan
Straight — are shapes that can be used to make up a block.

whenever a design folder is accessed. You can filter the contents of the display window
to show only certain file types. See Viewing designs in folders for details.

to show only certain file types. See Viewing designs in folders for details.
