Glossary

This glossary contains many embroidery and computing terms which you may meet
while using your BERNINA artista software.
Active window: The active window is one to

which the next command or action will apply. If
a window is ‘active’, its title bar changes color to
differentiate it visually from other open
windows.

Alphabet: A set of characters of the same

design or style including letters, numbers and
typographical symbols.

Anchor point: A fixed point used when

rotating, scaling, skewing or mirroring a design.

Anti-aliasing: A software technique similar to

Artwork: Bitmap image used as a backdrop for
digitizing.

Artwork preparation: The Reduce Colors
feature automatically reduces color blocks in
bitmap images to a single color, removing
anti-aliasing and noise. You can let the software
reduce the color count automatically or specify
a precise number. The latter is useful if you want
to match design colors to an exact number of
thread colors.
ARX: BERNINA artista Cross Stitch format.

dithering which is used to soften hard outlines
where color blocks intersect. It produces
smoother outlines by ‘blurring’ the pixels where
colors join.

Auto Appliqué: Auto Appliqué is an embroidery

Appliqué: Decoration or trimming cut from

Auto Digitizer: Digitize complete images

one fabric piece and stitched to another to add
dimension. APPLIQUE The use of fabrics sewn
onto one another for decoration that adds
dimension and texture. Designs with applique
are economical because they reduce the
amount of embroidery stitches needed to fill the
design area.

ARQ: BERNINA Quilter format.
ART: BERNINA artista format.
Artistic View: Artistic View: a realistic, ‘three

dimensional’ preview of a stitched embroidery
design.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

object associated with an appliqué which
provides automatic stitching to place it, tack it
down, and cover its edges.

automatically with Auto Digitizer. Auto Digitizer
automatically converts artwork to embroidery
objects and generates stitches.

Auto Jump: The Auto Jump feature preserves

long stitches in an object by splitting them into
a series of jumps. This prevents the machine
from inserting unwanted needle penetrations
when the maximum frame movement is
exceeded.

Auto-sequencing: The Auto-sequencing
feature lets you digitize like objects — e.g. the
fingers of a hand — without having to think
about the most efficient stitching sequence and
joins.

493

Glossary

Backdrop: A ‘backdrop’ is an electronic drawing
which you can use to digitize designs on-screen.
Two types can be used — vector or bitmap. You
can insert them from various file sources, or you
can copy and paste them via the Windows
clipboard.

Blanket stitch : Stitch used for edging
applique. Composed of running stitches in an Eor comb-shape.

Background: The area inside the active

Block: The basic unit of a quilt top.

window.

Backing: The ‘backing’ is the woven or

non-woven material used beneath an item or
fabric being embroidered to provide support and
stability. It can be hooped with the item or
placed between the machine throat plate and
hooped garment. It is available in various
weights and types such as cutaway, tearaway
and soluble.

Backstitch: Back stitch is a stitch which can be

used as a delicate outline. This stitch follows
intricate curves well if the stitches are worked in
small and in an even manner in order to follow
the flow of the curve.

Backup: The copying of files onto floppy disk or
other media so that the data is secure. Usually
two copies are made and kept separately.

Baseline: The imaginary line on which the

letters of an alphabet sit. (Only descenders
extend below it.)

Baseline Arc Angle: The Baseline Arc Angle

value constrains an arc to a specified number of
degrees.

Batting: A layer of padded material between

the front and back fabric to add thickness and
substance.

Bitmap: A picture or other graphic made up of

dots rather than of shape outlines or vectors.
Typically produced by paint programs, bitmaps
have file extensions such as BMP.

Blackwork: Blackwork gets its name from the

black silk thread traditionally used in this form
of embroidery. It can be used to decorate

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

articles such as hankies, table napkins, table
clothes, and doilies.

BMP: Windows Bitmap image format. See also
Bitmap.
Border: A border is a strip of fabric that is
joined to the inner quilt to enhance it.

Bounding outline: The dotted rectangle that
appears when you select a range of items.

Candlewicking : A traditional white-on-white

embroidery technique, usually done on white
linen or cotton fabric with heavy cotton threads.

Checkbox: A small square box that appears in

a dialog and that can be selected or cleared.
When the checkbox is selected, a tick appears in
the box. A checkbox represents an option that
you can set.

Click: To press and release the left mouse
button.

Clipboard: A temporary storage area in

memory for what was last cut or copied.
Stitching on the clipboard can be pasted into
designs any number of times.

Close button: For Windows, the small box with

an ‘X’ in it, to the right of the group of three at
the right of the title bar. Click the Close button to
close a window or an application.

Color palette: The color palette contains a

selection of thread colors tailored for each
design. This color scheme, or ‘colorway’,
represents the actual thread colors in which a
design will be stitched.

COM1/ COM2/COM3/COM4: A standard serial
port used as a connection point for peripherals.
Other ports may be present if the appropriate
internal option cards have been installed. The

494

Glossary
computer must be informed which port is being
used by which peripheral.

Command: A word or phrase, usually found in
a menu, that you can choose in order to carry
out an action.
Command button: A button in a dialog. It
carries out or cancels the selected action. Two
common command buttons are Cancel and OK.
Condensed format: Method of digitizing in

which a proportionate number of stitches are
placed between defined points after a scale has
been designated. With a machine that can read
condensed format, the scale, density, and stitch
lengths in a design may be changed. See
expanded format.

Configuration: The size and type of computer

Converter box: A converter box acts like a

translator from one memory card format to
another, via computer. Various companies offer
this technology under names like the Magic
Box™ from Oklahoma Embroidery. They
translate designs from memory cards in other
formats or from a hard drive, disk, or CD and
write the design to a blank card.

Copy: To place a copy of a selection onto the
clipboard. See Duplicate.
Cover stitch: The cover stitch is the Satin

border around an appliqué shape. You can
change the width of the cover stitch, and offset
it to the inside or outside of the digitized outline.

CPU: Central Processing Unit.
Cross stitch: Two stitches that cross to form

hardware. Can also be used to mean the options
provided with your software.

an X.

Confirmation message: A message displayed

artwork or manipulating existing patterns.

by the software asking you if you are sure you
want to proceed. For example, when you want
to delete a design.

Custom designs: Designs created by digitizing
Customizing: See Editing.
Cut: An editing function. To delete a selection

Connector stitches: Connector stitches link

objects in a design. They can be run stitches or
jumps.

from a design. The deletion is stored on the
clipboard and can be pasted back into the same
or a different design in a chosen location.

Connectors: Hardware devices to connect

Default values: Values that are automatically

cables to ports. If the connection is male, the
port is female, and vice versa. The wiring
configuration of each device is determined by its
function.

used unless you override them with special
values.

Control points: Control points are used to
modify object shapes, stitch angles and entry
and exit points. You can change the shape of an
object by moving, adding or deleting control
points on the outline. For some objects, you can
also change control points from corner points to
curves.

Design card: A type of disk used to store
embroidery designs.

Conversion software: Programs that read

Destination folder: The directory or folder to

information, other than from a card, and
translate it from one sewing machine format to
another.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Density (stitch): Number of stitches per given
area (or stitch lines per distance in a fill).

Design sequence: See Stitching Sequence.
Desktop: The screen background for Windows
on which windows, icons and dialogs appear.

which you intend to copy or move one or more
files.

495

Glossary

Dialog: A box that either requests or provides
information. Many dialogs present options to
choose among before a command is carried out.
Some dialogs present warnings or explain why a
command cannot be completed.
Digitize: Conversion of artwork into a series of
commands read by an embroidery machine’s
computer via a card. A modern term for
punching, it is a method of programming a
design. Artwork is converted into a series of
commands to be read by an embroidery
machine’s computer.

Disk drive: Computers usually have two types

of disk drives. A hard disk which usually
supports the mass storage of information and
applications. The other type is a floppy disk
drive for removable data. One or more floppy
disk drives are usual. The floppy disk drive is
conventionally called the A: drive and the hard
disk is the C: drive.

Display: The part of computer which includes
the screen. Also known as the monitor.
Dithering: Dithering is a software technique

which combines existing colors in a
checkerboard arrangement of pixels. It is
typically used to simulate colors that are
missing from an image palette. A type of optical
illusion created by placing two pixels of different
color next to each other. The human eye
automatically combines the two colors into a
third color.

Download: The process of transferring a copy
of a design file from a remote computer or the
internet or to an embroidery machine.
Drag: Hold the (left) mouse button and move
the mouse. Typically used for moving something
on the screen.
Duplicate: When an object is duplicated, it is
not copied to the clipboard. This leaves the
clipboard free for you to cut or copy other
objects.

Edge Walk underlay: Edge Walk underlay

places stitches around the edge of an object.
Use when digitizing large shapes.

Editing: Changing aspects of a design using a
computerized editing program. Software
capability that allows the user to manipulate
elements of a design, combine designs and
lettering, designs, move points and stitches.
Functions vary by manufacturer.

Editing: Changing aspects of a design using a
computerized editing program. Software
capability that allows you to manipulate
elements of a design, combine designs and
lettering, designs, move points and stitches.

Elastic Lettering: Apply Elastic Lettering

special effects to lettering objects to make them
bulge, stretch or compress.

Embroidery: Decorative stitching on fabric.

Dongle: Security device required to run the

Enlargement : The technical drawing usually 6
times larger than the original design, indicating
the stitches to be digitized. See Cartoon.

Dots per inch (dpi): A measurement of screen
or printer resolution; the number of dots in a
line one inch long. Abbreviated dpi.

Entry Point: Point where thread enters an
embroidery object.

Double-click: Click the left mouse button twice

existing objects are the settings stored with
each object in the design. You can make the
properties of a selected object into new current
properties or new defaults. You can also apply
current properties to existing objects.

BERNINA artista software.

without moving the mouse. Double-clicking
carries out an action such as opening an icon.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Existing object properties: Properties of

496

Glossary

Exit point: Point where thread leaves an
embroidery object.

Expanded files: See Stitch files.
Expanded format: A design program in which
individual stitches in a design have been
specifically digitized for a certain size. Designs
punched in this format cannot generally be
enlarged or reduced more than 10% to 20%
without distortion, because stitch count remains
constant. See condensed format.
Extension: The period and three letters at the
end of a filename. An extension identifies the
kind of information a file contains, e.g., BMP.
Fabric stretch: Embroidery stitches pull the

fabric inwards where the needle penetrates.
This can cause the fabric to pucker, and gaps to
appear in the embroidery. Use automatic pull
compensation to counter this effect by
‘overstitching’ outlines of filled shapes.

Facing/Stabilizer/Topping: Material hooped or
placed on top of fabric with a nap or surface
texture to contain the nap and hold the
embroidery stitches above it.
File: A named collection of specifically related

information stored on a disk. Designs that have
been saved are stored as files.

Filename: The name of a file, including the

Gradient Fill: Gradient Fill varies the stitch

spacing between dense and open fill, producing
shading and color effects which are difficult to
do manually.

Grayscale: A grayscale picture is made up of

254 different shades of gray, plus solid black
and solid white for a total of 256 different tones.
Black and white photographs are actually
grayscale.

Grid/gridlines: Grid lines provide visual cues to
help you accurately place a design. When you
start the software for the first time, the grid
lines appear by default.
Guide run: A layer of run stitches around the
outline of an appliqué object. It is the first
appliqué layer stitched and is used to position
appliqué shapes on the background material.

Hoop: Wood, plastic or steel device used to
tightly grip the fabric and stabilizer between an
inner and outer ring. Attaches to machine’s
frame. Designed to hold fabric taut against the
machine bed for embroidery.
Hooping: The process of placing the fabric

and/or stabilizer into the embroidery hoop. One
of the most common reasons for a poorly
stitched design is improper hooping.

Icon: Pictorial representation of an option

extension, e.g. dog.ART.

available from a menu on the machine screen or
software system.

Fill stitch: Series of running stitches

Image editing program: Software application

commonly used to cover large areas. Different
fill patterns can be created by altering the angle,
length and repeat sequence of the stitches.

Folder : A collection of files and subfolders that
are stored together on a disk. Part of structure
for organizing files on a disk.

Font: A set of characters, including letters,

numbers and other typographic symbols, of the
same design and style. Also called Alphabet,
even if it includes non-letter characters. See
also alphabets and lettering.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

that uses pixels so the images are bitmapped
images. You can edit the image pixel by pixel
using paintbrushes, erasers and spraypaint
tools. Examples of image editing programs are
Adobe Photoshop, Jasc PaintShop Pro and Corel
Photo-Paint.

Imported stitches: Designs read from stitch or
‘expanded’ files, and may or may not have
outlines recognized, but stitches have not been
regenerated through stitch processing.

497

Glossary

Jump: A hoop movement without a needle

Monogram: Embroidered design composed of
one or more letters, usually the initials of a
name.

Justification: The position of lettering on the

Mouse: A device, equipped with control buttons
and designed to roll about on the table next to
the keyboard. As the mouse moves, its circuits
relay signals that move a pointer on the screen.

penetration, commonly used to get from one
point in a design to another.
embroidery baseline.

Lacework: Lacework: an open square

trellis-like pattern fill which gives objects a lacy
look.

Lettering: Embroidery using letters or words.

Lettering commonly called ‘keyboard lettering’
may be created from pre-defined alphabet
styles or fonts, allowing variance of size, height,
spacing, density and other characteristics.

Magic Wand: Create an embroidery design by
automatically digitizing blocks of color in a
picture.

Maximize button: For Windows, the small box
in the center of the group of three at the right of
the title bar. Click the Maximize button to enlarge
a window to its maximum size.
Memory: The place in the computer’s system
unit that stores information while you are
working with it. If you exit without saving
information in memory, it will be lost.
Menu bar: The row of text options across the
top of the screen. Items can be selected to
display drop-down menus.
Minimize button: For Windows, the small box

to the left of the group of three at the right of
the title bar. Click the Minimize button to reduce
a window to its minimum size.

Minimum stitch length: The minimum

movement of the hoop. It is measured between
needle penetration points.

Mirror: A method of reproducing selected

objects as if they were a reflection of the
original. The location and orientation of the
mirrored stitches are determined by location
and angle of the axis of reflection relative to the
position of the original stitches.

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

Native file format: A design that is saved in

the same format as the program you are
working in, is saved in native file format or to an
embroidery machine. If it is saved in another
format is known as a non-native format.

Object: In embroidery design terms, a single
‘piece’ of the design created at one time, with a
tool. An object has many properties, such as its
size, color, sequence in the design, stitch type
and values, including the rules for stitching.
Outline: Outline or ‘condensed’ designs are
high-level formats which generally contain
design information, objects, logical functions as
well as stitch data. These formats are suited to
later design modification.
Outline recognition: When you convert a

stitch file to outline format, BERNINA artista
reads stitch data stitch-by-stitch according to
the needle penetration points. It recognizes
stitch types, spacing and length values, stitch
effects, and can determine object outlines.

Outline stitch: Stitch such as Run or Satin
used to outline an embroidery object.

Pan: Use to view parts of a design which are not
currently visible in the design window.

Parallel port: A connection on a computer,

usually LPT1, where you plug in the cable for a
parallel printer and/or a dongle.

Partial appliqué: Appliqué objects with partial
cover stitching to create an overlapping effect
without doubling-up borders.
Paste: To insert an object, which has previously
been placed on the clipboard by cutting or

498

Glossary
copying selected objects, into a design. You can
paste from the clipboard as many times as you
like.

Patch: A single piece of fabric sewn with others
into a set arrangement to form a patchwork
block.

Patchwork: The composite of pieces sewn

together to form a large piece, such as a quilt.

Patchwork block: A collection of patches sewn

together, usually forming a regular shape such
as a rectangle. These are then sewn together to
make a quilt.

Piecing: The business of sewing patches
together into patchwork blocks.

PDF: Portable Document Format. Used to view
the Online Manual in Acrobat Reader.

Peripherals: Any device connected to a
computer which is to some degree controlled by
the computer, e.g. an embroidery machine or
printer.
PhotoSnap: A technique for creating
embroidery designs directly from photographs
and other bitmap images. PhotoSnap designs
consist of rows of stitches of varying spacing
values. The effect resembles the output of a line
printer.
Pixel: A dot. For example, dots of light that
make up the picture on a computer screen. The
more pixels there are in a given area — that is,
the smaller and closer together they are — the
higher the resolution.
Pointer: A part of the screen display, the
pointer can take various shapes. It is moved by
moving the mouse and can be used to point to
anything on the screen to make selections and
indicate points for input. It also indicates when
the computer is working and no input is
possible.
Port: A connection on a computer where you
plug in the cable that carries data to another

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

device. Ports which are used to attach
peripherals have names like COM1 or LPT1 so
that you can specify where the peripherals are
attached.

Print Preview: Use to preview design and
design information before printing a Production
Worksheet. The design is displayed as it will be
printed.
Program: A computer program used for a

particular kind of work, such as word processing
or database management.

Pull compensation: Automatic Pull

Compensation calculates any necessary
overstitching automatically. This allows you to
digitize outlines without having to modify the
artwork first. After digitizing, the design can be
automatically adjusted for shrinkages of
different fabric.

Quilting: In general, the process of making a

quilt; in specific, the stitching of patterns into
the quilt layers to add strength and decoration
to the quilt - top, batting and backing -- to form
decorative patterns on the surface of the quilt
and to hold the layers together.

Quilt top: A collection of patches sewn

together, usually forming a regular shape such
as a rectangle and may include borders and
sashes.

Read: To open a design which has been written
on a design card or to an embroidery machine.

Reader/Writer: A device that allows you to
download/upload designs to and from a design
card. Cards can be purchased from your dealer
or from designers on the net. Cards can have
designs on them or are blank for you to
read/write from/to. The cards are inserted into
the embroidery module attached to your sewing
machine.
Redraw: The screen display is refreshed. This is
useful when parts of the display have become
obscured in the course of editing.

499

Glossary

Registration: Correct registration is achieved
when all stitches and design elements line up
correctly.

Relative: The distance the pointer is from a

previously marked point, or from the previous
stitch point.

Resequence: You can change the position of a
selected object by cutting it, then pasting it
somewhere else in the sequence, or by using
the Resequence command. You can also
resequence objects by color or using the
Color/Object List.
Resizing: The ability to scale a design to

different sizes. May cause density problems
unless stitch count can be altered.

RGB: The acronym stands for red, green, and
blue. RGB is the system used by computer
monitors to create color.

Right-click: To press and release the right
mouse button.

Sash: A fabric strip that separates blocks in a
setting, framing them and making the quilt
larger.

Satin stitch: Satin stitch is well-suited to
stitching narrow columns and shapes, where the
length of each stitch forms the width of the
column. Because there are generally no needle
penetrations breaking up the fill, Satin stitch
creates a glossy effect.

allows you to use artwork without recreating the
design.

Scroll bar: The bar at the bottom and right
edge of a window whose contents are not
entirely visible. Each scroll bar contains a small
box, called a scroll box, and two scroll arrows to
allow different types of scrolling.
Select: To highlight an object or group of
objects ready for editing. Only the selected
items will be edited.
Selection handle: Eight small squares that

appear symmetrically at the corners and edges
of a bounding box around a selected shape.

Serial port: A connection point on a computer,

usually COM1, where you plug a plotter or
another serial communications devices, such as
a modem.

Shortcut key: A key stroke or a series of

keystrokes you can use to perform a task
instead of using the mouse. For example, Ctrl + C
for the Copy command.

Slow Redraw: Use to redraw your design

slowly. Slow Redraw lets you view the stitching
and color sequence of a design in slow motion.

Software: The programs which run your

computer.

Specialty fill: Born of recent technology, a fill

Save: Use to save the current design.

stitch capability that produces a fill with a ‘relief’
or motif design within the fill-stitch area, e.g.
Pattern Fill.

Scalability: Ability to enlarge or reduce a

Stabilizer: Woven or nonwoven material used

design. In expanded format, most scaling is
limited to 10 to 20%, because the stitch count
remains constant despite final design size. In
condensed formats, on the other hand, scale
changes may be more dramatic, because stitch
count and density may be varied.

Scanner: A device that converts physical
images into digital form so that they can be
stored and manipulated by computer. Scanning

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

underneath the item or fabric being
embroidered to provide support and stability.
Available in various weights and types such as
cutaway, tearaway and washaway. Also know as
backing. The more stitches your design has, the
more backing you will need. Professional
embroiderers use tear away stabilizers for
woven fabrics and cut-away stabilizers for knits.

500

Glossary

Stemstitch: A detail stitch that can be used to
outline items or fill in areas. It is used for stems
and vines with other decorative stitches.
Step stitch: Series of parallel stitches
commonly used to cover large areas. Different
step patterns can be created by altering the
angle, the length and repeat sequence of the
stitches.

Stitching sequence: The embroidery objects in
a design form a stitching sequence. Initially,
objects are stitched in the order in which they
were created. You can change the position of a
selected object by cutting it, then pasting it
somewhere else in the sequence, or by using
the Resequence command.

Stippling: A method of creating a fill made of
run stitches which meander around within a
border. Often used in quilting.

Stock designs: Embroidery designs readily
available on disks or brand-specific cards.
Digitized embroidery designs that are
commercially available for general use by
embroiderers.

Stitch: A stitch is one needle penetration; also

System requirements: What your computer’s

used to refer to the thread laid down from one
needle penetration to the next.

hardware and software, operating together, can
support. System requirements are printed on

Stitch count: The number of stitches in a

to a background fabric before cover stitching is

the stitch count does not change, the density
Template: Templates are special files used to

Stitch length: Stitch length is the distance

maximum stitch length, the length is measured
Textured Edge: Use Textured Edge to create
according to the X and Y co-ordinates,

BERNINA® artista Online Manual

501

Glossary
stitches unraveling. You can adjust connector
settings to automatically add tie-offs, or add
them yourself. You can also include trim
functions so machines with trimmers cut the
thread automatically.

Titlebar: The horizontal bar located at the top
of a window and containing the title of the
window. On many windows, the title bar also
contains the Control menu box and Maximize
and Minimize buttons.

Topping: Material hooped or placed on top of

fabrics that have definable nap or surface
texture, such as corduroy and terry cloth, prior
to embroidery. The topping compacts the wale
or nap and holds the stitches above it. Includes
a variety of substances, such as plastic wrap,
water-soluble plastic ‘foil’ and open-weave
fabric that has been chemically treated to
disintegrate with the application of heat. Also
known as facing.

placed automatically under the column (satin)
or fill stitch areas of your embroidery design.

USB port: Universal Serial Bus Connector, a
connection on a computer where you plug in the
dongle.
Wave Effect: With Wave Effect you can curve
Closed Fill stitches along a digitized line to
create flowing stitch effects. The stitches follow
the digitized line but maintain uniform density
and needle penetration patterns.
Write: To send and store design information to
a design card or to an embroidery machine.

Zigzag underlay: Zigzag and Double Zigzag
underlay stitching is used to support wide
columns.

Traveling: You can check a design’s stitching

sequence by ‘traveling’ through it by stitches, or
objects.

TWAIN: An industry standard which allows
devices (such as scanners) to communicate
directly with design and layout programs (such
as BERNINA artista). Both device and program
must be TWAIN-compliant. This lets you use any
TWAIN-compliant scanner with BERNINA
artista.
Underlay margin: The distance between an
object outline and the edge of the underlay.
Increase this margin to prevent underlay
stitches from extending outside the cover
stitches.

Underlay stitch: Stitches laid down before
other design elements to help stabilize fabrics
The stitching action that will attach the backing
to the fabric being embroidered. It also supports
the top embroidery for a more lofty, dimensional
look. Underlay stitches are made up of a series
of single run stitches, usually with a very short
stitch length, and are digitized manually or
